<objective>
The host-CA end-to-end test of CA rotation (D-14, KEY-08), split off 03-12f by the owner on 2026-11-20: the proof, against real sshd and real clients, that the host-CA runbook gate is the right one, and the runbook text that states the gate.

Purpose: a host-CA switch reaches a client only through that client's own refresh, and a host moves to the new host CA only at its own renewal. A client that has not refreshed since ceremony 1 keeps working right after the switch and fails weeks later, host by host. This plan shows both halves in one test and writes the gate the owner chose into every text that says when a host-CA switch is safe. Nothing downstream depends on it: 03-13 and 03-15 depend on 03-12f only.

Output:
- TestHostCARotation in test/e2e/hostca_rotation_test.go, and the two shared helpers of test/e2e/ca_rotation_test.go parameterised by role;
- the e2e-rotation lane's `-run` list, PASS loop and comment, and CONTRIBUTING's two lane rows;
- the host-CA text of ca-rotation.md (the header, "Before ceremony 2", "The host CA", "Run in CI", "What changes for nobody"), enroll-user.md item 4a, one sentence in machine-identities.md "Renew a machine certificate", the host-role correction of signer-install.md "Install a CA switch: `--previous`", and their pins; the plan-number allowlist of `no_plan_numbers` shrunk to 03-13 and 03-16;
- this plan's text (replacing the early text on main), ROADMAP and STATE.

Each mutant is applied to the working tree by one exact, unique replacement, run with its exact command, and the file restored with `git checkout -- <file>` and hash-checked before the next: `sha256sum` of the file before the mutation and after the restore must be equal, and `git status --short -- cmd internal test docs .github` must list only the intended files. Every mutant command carries `-timeout 5m`, except the local end-to-end control and the three end-to-end mutants of Task 2 (M371, M372, M373), which carry `-timeout 12m`: their whole test runs, its passing run with every wait at its limit is about 515 s and a failing one about 575 s ("The test's timing", the census of bounded waits), so 5, 8 or 10 minutes could cut a run before its kill or leave it a margin of under half a minute over a figure that holds two estimated tool-time terms; 12 minutes (720 s) leaves about 145 s over the failing figure; this is the mutant rule's form for these three. A CI guard (the lane's PASS loop) carries a recorded negative proof in place of a test mutant.

## Owner decisions (final; do not ask again)

All answers are Daniel's.

- **The split (2026-11-20).** 03-12f keeps the TPM and PIV generations; this plan takes TestHostCARotation, its runbook text (the host-CA section of ca-rotation.md and the enroll-user.md paragraph), the e2e.yml lane edit and CONTRIBUTING. It depends on 03-12f at wave 39; 03-13 to 03-16 moved one wave.
- **The host-CA runbook gate (2026-11-14; the design record's option A).** Install ceremony 2 for the host role no earlier than the ceremony-1 install plus the user profile's max TTL; automation and `--no-known-hosts` users run `known-hosts sync` first; the runbook states that a client that never refreshes cannot be rotated safely; TestHostCARotation documents the failure. Option B (a tool check in `root sign --ca-switch host`) was not chosen, so no tool enforces the gate.
  **Recorded note on this decision (orchestrator, 2026-11-21; reported to the owner, not an open question, and no task waits on it).** The decision's sentence presumed that automation can run `known-hosts sync`. The tool needs an admin key: `keyroster known-hosts sync` opens an admin session first (cmd/keyroster/knownhosts.go:1059), which refuses without an admin key from the root-signed policy loaded in ssh-agent (cmd/keyroster/dirpipe.go:68-74; the text at cmd/keyroster/user.go:39-40), and machine-identities.md sets an automation host up with no admin key (:72-75). The orchestrator ruled that the runbook text states who can run what with the tools as they are: a user refreshes with a `keyroster login` without `--no-known-hosts`, an admin with `known-hosts sync`, and an account with neither is refreshed by an admin who runs sync as that account, or it is a client that never refreshes. An account has refreshed when its managed `keyroster_known_hosts` holds the new host CA's line (orchestrator's ruling of the same day); the outcome line a command prints is no such test. A refresh path of automation's own is a product item outside this plan.
- **A refresh path of automation's own (2026-11-21).** Daniel said yes to a way of automation's own to refresh host trust in the product (for example `keyroster machine renew` also refreshing host trust), as a plan of its own. It is outside this plan: the gate's text here states the tools as they are, and that later plan changes it.
- **The rotation lane's time (2026-11-17).** If an `e2e-rotation` lane's `go test` takes more than 900 s, the executor stops and reports; no timeout is raised, now or in any fix round.
- **The set (2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14).** Hardware optional; the product path only (a real `keyroster known-hosts sync`, the agent's own lazy renewal; no test-only path in product code); every refusal has a positive control.
- **Delivery (2026-10-29 and 2026-11-02).** The plan lands in the same PR as its change, with its ROADMAP and STATE rows; signed commits authored as Daniel with keyroster-bot as committer and pusher; no auto-merge; Daniel squash-merges.

None open. The choices below are the planner's and change no owner decision.

## Notes for the orchestrator's report to the owner

- **The three budget figures** ("The budget"): a typical run lands the rotation lanes at about 681 s (9.5p1) and 672 s (10.5p1); the slowest passing run counted with the observed durations of the setup and the tail and the one re-anchor at about 860 s and 851 s; and the sum with every wait loop of the setup, of c1 and of the tail at its limit, the cleanups included, with the observed tool time of the setup and of the tail, at about 1055 s and 1046 s, above 900 s; it is the sum of the waits that "The test's timing" lists in its census. That last figure is reached only when every wait of one run sits at its limit; the owner's rule then stops the executor on the measured time of the PR's run, the run is reported, and no timeout is raised and no bound is tightened for it.
- **The lane has room for this test but not for further long tests.** A later plan that adds rotation tests needs a lane of its own, which means new required checks and a ruleset change: the owner's call.
- **The host-CA gate's decision met a fact it did not account for** (the recorded note under "Owner decisions"): `known-hosts sync` needs an admin key, so automation is refreshed by an admin or it is a client that never refreshes. A refresh path of automation's own would be a product item.

## Planner decisions (Claude's discretion), recorded with their reasons

- **B's refusal and every login of the test offer `trustCertAlgs`** (`ssh-ed25519-cert-v01@openssh.com,ssh-ed25519`, test/e2e/hosttrust_test.go:44), not the certificate algorithm alone, which the specification's step 8 names. The house form uses it for every connection a test expects ssh to refuse (hosttrust_test.go:38-43; TestHostCertIssueAndServe/other_name_refused, test/e2e/hostcert_test.go:332-335: "OpenSSH 10.x drops the retry otherwise"), and it is proven to print `Host key verification failed` on both builds in CI (TestHostCertNoTOFU's refusals, hosttrust_test.go:350-352 and :445-447). It does not weaken a positive: the managed known_hosts file holds `@cert-authority` lines only (cmd/keyroster/knownhosts.go:854), so the plain key can never be accepted, and a login that succeeds has accepted the certificate. A and B use the same offer, the same user certificate, the same host and the same ssh configuration apart from the known_hosts file, so the file is the only difference between them.
- **Each login also names the CA that signed the certificate it met.** `ssh -v` logs the served certificate as `Server host certificate: ...` before it checks it (the house assertion is the bare words, hostcert_test.go:317). The test requires, for every login, that this line names the expected host CA's `SHA256:` fingerprint, and logs it. That the line carries the CA's fingerprint on both builds is not anchored in this repository (no base log holds the line: 0 hits in both base E2E runs); the local pre-check is where it is first read. If either build's line does not name the CA's fingerprint, the executor stops and reports with the line as printed; the fallback, for the orchestrator to rule on, is the keyscan bracket alone. The keyscan bracket stays in any case: `ssh-keyscan -c` before and after each login that claims a generation shows which certificate sshd served (hostcert_test.go:151).
- **enroll-user.md gets item 4a, not a new item 5.** Items 5 to 9 are referenced by number: enroll-user.md:565 and :713 ("item 8"), host-setup.md:831 ("enroll-user.md, item 8", a file outside files_modified), and the `htrbWant` labels of cmd/keyroster/hosttrust_runbook_test.go:117-121. `**4a.` occurs 0 times in the file at the base. The specification says "an item after 4"; 4a is that item without renumbering.
- **The anchors of the test's timing are the previous issuance, never "from now"** ("The test's timing"). The earliest renewal request is at the served certificate's issuance plus 52 s (hostcert.go:556, :549, :564-568); the house bound of 51 s (hostcert_test.go:408) keeps one more second. Around c1, the two stops start by c1 plus 40 s and serve again by plus 50 s. Around r1 every step has its own bound so that all of them, the quiet check included, end before plus 52 s, derived from the real path from serving again to the commit being seen ("The test's timing", Back-off): the switch starts by r1 plus 19 s and serves again by plus 23 s (8 s after the latest fresh observation of r1), step 6 is done by plus 28 s, the bundle-3 commit is seen by plus 44 s (21 s after serving again, for a path of about 20 s), and the quiet check starts at plus 45 s and ends by plus 50 s, each keyscan try of it bounded by the time left. An anchor certificate (c1 or r1) observed more than 15 s after its issuance is replaced by its own renewal; **one re-anchor per test**: a second late anchor, of either certificate, fails the test. The re-anchor keeps a late observation from failing a required check; it is not dropped, and no wait is tightened to make a budget figure fit (orchestrator's ruling, 2026-11-21).
- **The test shows directly that the host stays quiet after it applies the switch.** At r1 plus 45 s, after the bundle-3 commit, three checks in this order. First, the host's attempts file, `<state>/hostcert-attempts.json` (`hostcert.AttemptsFile`, internal/agent/hostcert/hostcert.go:78), read from `eh.state` and parsed with `hostcert.ParseAttempts` (:350), holds no request whose `created_at` (`PendingRequest.CreatedAt`, :302-306, the agent's Unix second, :612) lies above r1's issuance second, and every key's `failures` is 0 and its `last_code` empty (both reset when a certificate is installed, internal/agent/apply/hostcert.go:782-783; a refused answer sets them, :633); its positive control is r1's own request, which the file still holds (the newest `MaxPending` are kept, `AddRequest`, hostcert.go:464-470), created after the issuance of the certificate r1 renewed and no later than r1's. Second, the drop-in names r1 alone and sshd serves r1. Third, the agent's run log holds no `WARNING host certificate answer for` line (written by internal/agent/apply/hostcert.go:603 through `logf`, apply.go:317-320, to the run command's stderr, cmd/keyroster-agent/run.go:93, which `startDaemon` captures into `eh.run`, test/e2e/harness_test.go:135-136), checked again after r2 for the whole test. The first check does not depend on the signer: the agent records every request it makes in the attempts file before it writes the request to its outbox (`requestHostCert`, apply/hostcert.go:908-927: `AddRequest` and `saveHostCertAttempts` at :925-926, the outbox write after them), in the same applier pass that applied the bundle, in this order: `Apply` writes the committed status at its end (apply.go:399-403); because the pass applied, `nextCheck` was zeroed (run.go:284), so the drift check `check()` runs next (run.go:320-322, `Check` with its own `sshd -T`, :210-217); then `hostCerts()` (:323-327), whose `requestHostCert` saves the request. The commit reaches the server in the heartbeat of the poll after the one that fetched the bundle, at least 4.17 s after it, and the quiet check reads the file at least 1 s after the commit was seen, so the margin between the status write and the read is that poll interval plus 1 s, which the drift check and the host-certificate step must fit in (both run `sshd -T` and local file work; the attempts file is written whole, temp file and rename, internal/agent/spool/spool.go:267-269, so the read never sees a partial file). Should the margin ever be missed under M373, the mutant still fails later and never passes: at step 7 (`no renewal installed by r1's issuance plus 75 s`, the refusal's 60 s back-off delaying the next request) or at step 8's whole-test log assertion; the record names the assertion that fired. An early request after the switch is therefore in the file whatever the signer answers: inside the signer's recent window it gets r1 back (internal/cert/hostrecent.go:62-64: a third of the 90 s cap; internal/signer/hostissue.go:162-170), which an agent that no longer trusts generation 1 refuses (`answer_unpinned_ca`, hostcert.go:699-700) and logs; after that window it gets a new certificate, which the drop-in check sees. The request's second lies above r1's issuance second, because the agent learns of bundle 3 only in a fetch poll after the one that carried r1's request, at least 4.17 s later (`NextPoll`, internal/agent/fetch/fetch.go:259-272: base minus base/6 at the least). The signer's own log is not needed for any of the three checks.
- **machine-identities.md and signer-install.md are pinned through `sectionWindow`.** machine-identities.md's phrases are pinned today only by the `machine_identities_pop` entry of `successorSigningSections` (cmd/keyroster/runbook_test.go:270), a list of signing sections; the new sentences are pinned inside `host_ca_gate` with a `signingSection` literal read by the same helper (`sectionWindow`, :282), so that list keeps its meaning.
- **Doctor after retire-key must exit 0 and print the line that only a complete run prints** (`OK switch_floor: host generation 2: the log records no certificate of this key; the earliest is the floor`; internal/doctor/doctor.go:356-359, the last block of `switchResults`), beside the absence of `draining_key_still_usable`. TestCARotationNoFailedLogin logs doctor's exit status and asserts neither (test/e2e/ca_rotation_test.go:808-812); the base logs show `doctor after retire-key exited 0` in all four rotation jobs, so exit 0 holds for the agent backend with its WARN lines.
- **Client B's remedy is part of the test.** After the refusal, B's known_hosts file is replaced by a byte copy of the file a fresh `known-hosts sync` writes, and B logs in again with nothing else changed. This shows that the refusal had one cause, the file. The specification calls it optional.
- **The test brings its own small helpers** (`hcr...`, free at the base): a stopped-signer helper with two deadlines from the anchor certificate, a bounded keyscan, a renewal wait bounded from the previous issuance, a sync that checks the CA lines, a login that checks the served CA, and a commit wait that polls `host status --json` every 2 s. `keyscanCert` (hostcert_test.go:151) is not used, because its ten tries of up to 15 s each could add 155 s to a failing run; the helper's tries are 5 s each within 15 s.
- **The two shared helpers are parameterised, not copied.** `caRotRetireKey` gains a `role` argument and `caRotDrainRE` becomes a function of the role, both byte-identical for `"user"`; the retire-key output parser is security-relevant parsing, so it exists once. Task 2's gate holds the diff of ca_rotation_test.go to the two helpers and their two call sites.
- **ca-rotation.md gets the gate in two more places.** "Before ceremony 2" is the operator's checklist before a switch, and "What changes for nobody" says that the clients keep their configuration; both get one sentence in the gate's words, pinned ("The sweep"). The user-role sections that "The host CA" does not repeat ("Wait for the hosts", the floor in "Ceremony 2", "The drain") stay as they are, because the runbook rotates "a CA key of a role (here `user`)" (ca-rotation.md:3); "The host CA" says what differs for the host role, each sentence with its anchor.
- **No new threat ID.** The test is the end-to-end leg of T-03-417 (a host-CA switch before clients refreshed; defined in 03-12d-PLAN.md:816). T-03-430 (a drained host CA staying in a client's known_hosts; 03-12d-PLAN.md:818) is untouched, because the test ends long before the drain.

## The fixed test, and what it must assert

TestHostCARotation (03-12f2, D-14, KEY-08), in test/e2e/hostca_rotation_test.go, first line exactly `//go:build e2e && e2erotation`. One enrolled host, `web1`, whose host certificate cap is 90 s (`withGenesisHostMaxTTL(t, 90*time.Second)`, as test/e2e/hostcert_test.go:398 does), the default user cap (`--user-max-ttl` 12 h, cmd/keyroster/root.go:143), so `joinHostCert`'s 1 h role (hostcert_test.go:54) and `issueAgentCert`'s 10 min certificate (test/e2e/agent_apply_test.go:383) work; `startServerEnv(t, serverOpts{PoPHosts: true, AgentListen: true, StoppableSigner: true})`; no `t.Parallel` and no goroutine (the genesis caps are package globals, test/e2e/bootstrap_test.go:64-67: "The e2e tests run one at a time"). The real home comes first: `home := requireRealHome(t)` (test/e2e/hosttrust_test.go:57), then an admin CLI with `admin.home = home` and `kr := hostTrustKR(t, env, admin)`, as TestHostCertNoTOFU does (hosttrust_test.go:286-291), because `keyroster known-hosts sync` refuses a `$HOME` that is not the account's home (cmd/keyroster/knownhosts.go:139). The host joins through `joinHostCert(t, env, kr, "web1")` (hostcert_test.go:51), whose fetcher polls every 5 s (`hostCertPoll`, :26); never `joinApproved` or `startEnrolled`, whose fetcher polls every second (test/e2e/agent_enroll_test.go:134-141).

The clients. Client A uses the live managed files: `cfgA := writeTrustConfig(t, "~/.ssh/"+trustSSHConfig, eh.sshd.port)` (hosttrust_test.go:120). Client B uses a copy of the managed known_hosts file taken right after the first sync, `bKH`, and `cfgB := writeTrustConfig(t, copyManagedConfig(t, sshDir, bKH, ""), eh.sshd.port)` (:230). Every login is `trustedHostLogin(t, cfg, login, "web1", src, keyAuth(c), "-o", "HostKeyAlgorithms="+trustCertAlgs)` (:142, :165, :44) with the one user certificate `c := issueAgentCert(t, env.Signer, "hc1", "role:ops")`; A and B log in from 127.0.0.1, and B's refusal comes from 127.0.0.2, a source of its own (OpenSSH 10.x PerSourcePenalties; the house pattern gives each refusal its own source). A successful login requires exit 0, `Server host certificate` and the expected CA's fingerprint on that line, and no `authenticity of host` or `Permanently added` text (`wantNoPrompt`, :170).

| Step | What is asserted | Bound (anchored) | Positive control | The misbehaviour that fails it, and how the plan shows it |
|------|------------------|------------------|------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|
| 0. Genesis certificate | The drop-in names one certificate c1 (`waitHostCerts`, hostcert_test.go:127); c1 is signed by host CA generation 1 (its `SignatureKey` equals `pubKey(t, readPub(t, sg.HostCAPub))` byte for byte), lives 90 s (`hostCertLifetime`, :142) and is what sshd serves (`hcrServes`) | within 60 s of `joinHostCert`'s return; c1 must be observed no later than its issuance plus 15 s, or the test spends its one re-anchor on c1's renewal | none needed | A cap that is not applied (lifetime not 90) or a drop-in sshd does not serve: in-test assertions |
| 1. Genesis sync | `keyroster known-hosts sync` exits 0 and prints `Host trust refreshed (directory v` (knownhosts.go:592, the outcome line, printed at the end); `caLines` (hosttrust_test.go:181) of `~/.ssh/keyroster_known_hosts` is exactly one line, host pattern `web1`, key generation 1. B's copy is taken now (`copyFile`, test/e2e/revoke_test.go:641) and equals A's file byte for byte | none (no wait) | B's copy equal to A's file at this point | B given the refreshed lines after all: mutant M371 (Task 2) must make step 8's refusal fail |
| 2. Prepare, ceremony 1, install | prepare `--role host --key <fp2>` (the key added to `sg.RoleAgent`) exits 0 and prints `role host generation 2 <fp2>` and the `prepare file: ` line (cmd/keyroster-signer/rotate.go:214, its last line); `caRotCeremony` with `--ca-next host=<file> --ca-next-fingerprint host=<fp2>` (test/e2e/ca_rotation_test.go:74; it checks root sign's two runs and `OK: successor of trust bundle`) prints `CA host gen 2 <fp2>: none -> next` exactly twice; install-bundle exits 0 with `installed bundle version 2`; the host's heartbeat reports bundle 2 committed (`Current.Bundle == 2` and `Heartbeat.Status.Committed(a.Current.Docs())`, internal/agent/heartbeat/heartbeat.go:578-581) | both stops start by c1's issuance plus 40 s and serve again by plus 50 s (`hcrStopped`); the commit within 45 s of the install | ceremony 1 runs while the signer serves | A renewal request that met a stopped signer: the helper's deadlines fail first, with the window named. A host that does not take bundle 2: the commit bound |
| 3. Refresh A | the sync now writes exactly two CA lines, both `web1`, keys generation 1 and generation 2 (as a set); A logs in, meeting a certificate by generation 1; B logs in too; `previous := sg.exportLog(t)` (bootstrap_test.go:382), taken after ceremony 1's install | none | B's login here and at step 6 is the control for B's refusal at step 8 | A sync that does not add generation 2: this step's line count. A that lacks the generation-2 line later: mutant M372 (Task 2) must make step 8's A login fail |
| 4. Anchor on a fresh renewal | r1 (`hcrNext`): serial above c1's, issued 51 s to 75 s after c1's issuance, signed by generation 1, 90 s, served; observed by its issuance plus 15 s, or replaced by its own renewal if the test's one re-anchor is still unspent (a second late anchor fails). Then at once ceremony 2: `caRotCeremony` from the version-2 directory with `--ca-switch host --drain-allowance 60s`; bundle 3's draining host generation 1 is trusted until its `issued_at` plus 450 s; both CA-change lines (`CA host gen 1 <fp1>: active -> draining, trusted until <t>; install by <issued_at + 60 s> (advice)` and `CA host gen 2 <fp2>: next -> active`) exactly twice | r1 installed by c1's issuance plus 75 s (c1's ValidBefore minus the missing margin of 15 s, hostcert.go:546; hostcert_test.go:408 and :411); a call that starts after that deadline still looks once (`waitHostCerts` checks before it tests its deadline, hostcert_test.go:129-136), and a late look is judged by the issuance gap and the freshness rule, never failed for the call's own lateness | none | A host that renews out of its schedule: the [51, 75) bound. A wrong allowance or cap in the switch: the trusted_until and display assertions |
| 5. The switch | with the signer stopped: install `--previous <the step-3 export>` exits 0 with `installed bundle version 3` and `drain host generation 1 until <t>; the last certificate the log records for it is valid until <v>` (internal/signer/drain.go:61), t equal to bundle 3's trusted_until, v equal to r1's ValidBefore in `trust.TimeFormat`, and v plus 300 s at or below t; `serve` exits non-zero naming `draining_key_still_usable` within its 10 s deadline (not by the deadline); `caRotRetireKey(t, sg, "host", fp1)` (exit 1 with `retire_key_incomplete`, the printed `ssh-add -d` lines run, then exit 0 with `retire-key: no draining key of role host is held`, cmd/keyroster-signer/rotate.go:96); `caRotAgentHolds` (ca_rotation_test.go:608); doctor exits 0, prints `OK switch_floor: host generation 2: the log records no certificate of this key; the earliest is the floor` and no `draining_key_still_usable`; then serve again | the stop starts by the earlier of r1's issuance plus 19 s and bundle 3's `issued_at` plus 50 s, and the signer serves again by r1's issuance plus 23 s | serve refuses before retire-key and serves after it (serve again is the helper's restart, which fails the test if serve does not come up) | A gen-1 certificate issued after the export that the binding misses, or a retire-key that leaves the key: the drain line, serve's refusal, the agent's listing and doctor |
| 6. The old key still serves | right after serve: sshd serves r1, A logs in meeting generation 1, B logs in meeting generation 1, sshd still serves r1, all done by r1's issuance plus 28 s; then the heartbeat reports bundle 3 committed, seen by r1's issuance plus 44 s | step 6 done by r1 plus 28 s (message: `step 6 ended at <t>, after r1 + 28 s`); the commit by r1 plus 44 s (message names the bound and that the quiet check needs it) | B's login here: B's failure comes with the renewal, not with the switch | B already refused at the switch, or a served certificate other than r1: the logins and the keyscan bracket |
| 6a. The host stays quiet | the test waits until r1's issuance plus 45 s, then: the host's attempts file (`<eh.state>/hostcert-attempts.json`, `hostcert.ParseAttempts`) holds no request created after r1's issuance second, and every key's `failures` is 0 and `last_code` empty; the drop-in names r1 alone (`installedHostCerts`, hostcert_test.go:93); sshd serves r1 (`hcrServes` until r1 plus 50 s, each try's context the smaller of 5 s and the time left, so the check ends by plus 50 s); and the agent's run log holds no refused host certificate answer yet | at r1 plus 45 s, at least 1 s after the bundle-3 commit was seen and before the earliest renewal request at plus 52 s | r1's own request in the attempts file, created after the issuance of the certificate r1 renewed and no later than r1's | A host that asks for a certificate at the switch: the attempts assertion, whatever the signer answered (the agent saves the request before it sends it). Its later effects are the second and third checks: a new certificate after the signer's recent window, which the drop-in names; r1 re-delivered inside the window, refused and logged, which the log assertion sees (again at step 8 for the whole test). Mutant M373 |
| 7. The renewal under the new key | r2 (`hcrNext`): serial above r1's, issued 51 s to 75 s after r1's issuance, signed by generation 2, 90 s, served | r2 installed by r1's issuance plus 75 s | none | A signer that still signs with generation 1: the CA assertion. A host off its schedule: the bound |
| 8. After the renewal | the agent's run log for the whole test (`eh.run`) holds no line with `WARNING host certificate answer for` (internal/agent/apply/hostcert.go:603); A logs in meeting generation 2; B, from 127.0.0.2, exits non-zero with `Host key verification failed`, its `Server host certificate` line naming generation 2, and no TOFU text; sshd still serves r2; then the remedy: a fresh sync still writes the two lines (generation 1 drains until t), B's file is replaced by a copy of the live file, and B logs in from 127.0.0.1 meeting generation 2 | none | A's login just before B's refusal, with the same offer, certificate, host and configuration; B's login after the remedy | B refreshed after all (M371): B is accepted, the refusal assertion fails. A without the generation-2 line (M372): A is refused, A's assertion fails. A host that asked early (M373) is stopped at step 6a; this step's whole-test log assertion backs step 6a up and does not run under M373 |
| 9. Audit and the lane's line | `auditVerify(t, sg, sg.exportLog(t))` (test/e2e/audit_test.go:28) exits 0, names `trust bundle v3`, and its `host certificates: <n>` line (the pattern of hostcert_test.go:434-439) has n at least 3; then the test logs exactly one line, `host CA rotation: the refreshed client accepted generation 1 and generation 2, the client that did not refresh was refused`, which Task 4's lane gate counts | none | — | A log that does not verify: the audit assertions |

The test does not wait for the host drain: trusted_until is bundle 3's issued_at plus 450 s, and the test ends at most about r1's issuance plus 100 s, where issued_at follows r1. So narrowing, which every admin `kr` call runs on the real `~/.ssh/keyroster_known_hosts` (cmd/keyroster/narrow.go:10, "never adds a line"), never removes the generation-1 line during the test, and B's copy lies outside the managed path. A host-only bundle change renders identical `user_ca_keys` on the hosts, because host CA keys are never rendered into a host's own files (internal/trust/castate.go:102: `for _, role := range []string{RoleUser, RoleMachine} {`), so the host's own trust files are no evidence of the switch; the heartbeat's committed bundle is.

**Every tool run checks its exit status and a line that only a complete run prints:** prepare (`prepare file: `), root sign and trust verify (`caRotCeremony`'s checks), install-bundle (`installed bundle version <n>`, and the drain line for the switch), serve (its refusal text, not its deadline), retire-key (both runs, `caRotRetireKey`), doctor (the `switch_floor` line of its last block), known-hosts sync (`Host trust refreshed (directory v`), ssh (`Server host certificate` with the CA, or `Host key verification failed`), ssh-keyscan (exit 0 and a parsed certificate), audit verify (`trust bundle v3` and the `host certificates:` line), and the copies (`copyFile` fails the test on error).

## The test's timing (every bound from the previous issuance, every constant anchored)

- **Renewal point.** `RenewAt` is `IssuedAt(c) + 2*v/3 - j` (internal/agent/hostcert/hostcert.go:556 and :568), j drawn from `[0, jitterMax(v))` (:564) with `jitterMax(v) = min(3600, v/10)` (:549). At v = 90 s (`hostCertLifetime`, the signer's `min(maxHostLifetime, cap)`, internal/signer/hostissue.go:29 and :146): RenewAt is the issuance plus 52 s to 60 s. `IssuedAt` is ValidAfter plus `Backdate = 300` (hostcert.go:55). The request then waits for the fetcher's next poll (5 s) and the applier's next round (1 s, agent_enroll_test.go:151): the base logs show renewals 55, 57, 60 and 61 s after the previous issuance (TestHostCertRenewAndRestart's `renewed: ... s apart` lines, E2E runs 44861507363 and 44867318533). The house bound is [51, 75) (hostcert_test.go:408).
- **Missing margin.** `MissingMargin(v) = min(60, v/6)` (hostcert.go:546): 15 s at 90 s, so the agent counts a certificate missing at its issuance plus 75 s, which is the upper bound of every renewal wait.
- **Back-off.** A failed request costs `BackoffMin = 60` s (hostcert.go:64, :593 when the certificate is within `jitterMax` of ValidBefore, and `min(60·2^(n-1), 3600)` otherwise, :596-598). A renewal that meets a stopped signer near the issuance plus 52 s would retry at about plus 112 s, after the certificate expired at plus 90 s. So every stopped window lies inside a quiet window: around c1 it starts by the issuance plus 40 s and serves again by plus 50 s; around r1 it starts by plus 19 s and serves again by plus 23 s, so that step 6 (by plus 28 s), the bundle-3 commit (seen by plus 44 s) and the quiet check (plus 45 s to plus 50 s) also end before the earliest request at plus 52 s. The r1 window is derived from four constraints, each met: every bound lies before plus 52 s; the 21 s from the latest serve (plus 23 s) to the commit bound (plus 44 s) cover the real path below; 8 s lie between the latest fresh observation of r1 (plus 15 s) and the serve bound (plus 23 s), 4 s for ceremony 2 and 4 s for the stopped window, which took about 23 ms and under 0.1 s in the base runs (the user test's `ceremony 2:` line 23 ms after the line before it, and `the switch: the signer was down for 72ms` and `57ms`, run 44867318533); and the quiet check ends by plus 50 s with each keyscan try's context the smaller of 5 s and the time left, before the earliest request. **The real path from serving again to the commit being seen**, from the code: the server answers the agents from a cached signer status up to 5 s old (`statusTTL`, internal/server/signer.go:372, `currentStatus` :435-443; the agent routes read it, internal/server/agent.go:308 and internal/server/directory.go:168-173), so bundle 3 reaches the agents' answers within 5 s of the signer serving again; the fetcher's next poll comes within 5 s plus up to 5/6 s of jitter (`NextPoll`, internal/agent/fetch/fetch.go:259-272; `hostCertPoll`); the applier picks the bundle up within its 1 s round (agent_enroll_test.go:151) and writes the committed status after the apply (internal/agent/apply/apply.go:399-403); the heartbeat goes out in each poll after the state fetch (`Once`, fetch.go:280-308), so the commit shows in the next poll's heartbeat, within another 5.83 s; and the test's own commit poll runs every 2 s. That is 5 + 5.83 + 1 + 5.83 + 2 = 19.7 s, plus the requests' and the `host status` call's own durations, counted at their observed size as every tool call here is: about 20 s, within the 21 s allowance. **The condition the allowance rests on:** the applier picks bundle 3 up and finishes its `Apply` (a new generation, the transition and the self-test, bounded only by `SelfTestTimeout`, 10 s by default, internal/agent/apply/apply.go:132-133, :330-352), writing the committed status (:399-403), before the fetcher's next poll sends its heartbeat, which can come as soon as 4.17 s after the poll that fetched the bundle. A host-only bundle renders the same files (castate.go:102), so no reload or widening wait applies and the apply is expected to take well under that, but no e2e test at the base installs a host-role CA bundle, so it is not measured. If pickup and apply take longer, the commit shows one poll later, about 23 s after serving again, and a correct product fails with `bundle 3 not committed by r1 + 44 s`, never a wrong pass. The local pre-check and the SUMMARY record, for bundle 2 and for bundle 3, the lag from the signer serving again to the commit being seen and from the install to the commit, so the real margin against the bounds is on record; no bound changes for it. The base reading of the user test (its switch bundle committed within about 11 s of its install, "Heartbeat" below) is an upper bound that includes a KRL change and is not this path's measure. The signer's stops in the user rotation test took 28 ms to 51 ms (the base logs: `the signer was down for 28ms`, `29ms`, `51ms`).
- **Fetcher poll and rate limit.** The host-certificate fetcher polls every 5 s (hostcert_test.go:26); each poll sends a state request and a heartbeat, so 24 requests a minute against the server's 30 a minute and burst 64 per agent (internal/server/agent.go:43-44; a 1 s fetcher stalls a long test, 03-12e2's deviation 9). The test's own `host status --json` loops run every 2 s (30 a minute) against the admin limit of 60 a minute and burst 20 (internal/server/ratelimit.go:60-61); `hostTrustKR` retries a rate-limited call (hosttrust_test.go:103-106).
- **Heartbeat.** A heartbeat goes with every poll, so the commit of a bundle shows within a few polls; the bound is 45 s (9 polls) from the install for bundle 2, and r1's issuance plus 44 s for bundle 3 (21 s after the latest serve, for the path of about 20 s above; the quiet check needs it before plus 45 s). A host-only bundle renders the same files (castate.go:102), so no widening wait applies; in the user test the switch bundle was committed within about 11 s of its install (the base logs, `the switch` then `after the switch`), a reading that includes `caRotRevoke` and its KRL wait before the bundle-3 wait (ca_rotation_test.go:824-831), so it bounds the path from above and does not isolate it.
- **The drain arithmetic.** Bundle 3's trusted_until is issued_at plus max(90, 90) plus 300 (`DrainMargin`, castate.go:122) plus the allowance of 60 s: issued_at plus 450 s (`DrainFloor`, castate.go:134-150). The last generation-1 certificate the log records is r1, valid until r1's issuance plus 90 s; ceremony 2 follows r1, so v plus 300 s is at most issued_at plus 390 s, below t: the binding (`InstallBundleWithPrevious`, drain.go:65-78) accepts the switch. An allowance of 0 would refuse it whenever the agent renews between issued_at and the install, which it does by itself; hence 60 s and the stop check at issued_at plus 50 s.
- **The anchors.** c1, r1 and r2 each carry the bound of the step that waits for them; the only "from now" bound is c1's 60 s after `joinHostCert` returns. `hcrNext` never fails for being called late: `waitHostCerts` looks once before it tests its deadline (hostcert_test.go:129-136), so a late call returns the installed renewal, which is then judged by its issuance gap. A call made after the previous issuance plus 75 s that finds a renewal 75 s or more after it has met a later renewal than the one it waits for (for example `hcrNext(c1)` after a bundle-2 commit near its limit and a slow step 3, which then returns r1's own renewal): it fails with a message that names the late call and the step before it, not the agent's schedule. If an anchor is observed more than 15 s after its issuance, the test spends its one re-anchor: it waits for that certificate's renewal and anchors on it. c1 is late when the first certificate's delivery was slow; r1 is late when steps 2 and 3 end after r1's issuance plus 15 s, which takes a bundle-2 commit (bounded at the install plus 45 s) or a step 3 that ends after about c1 plus 67 s at the earliest r1. A second late anchor, of either certificate, fails the test.

Tool calls are counted at their observed durations: runBin and env.keyroster carry 60 s contexts (test/e2e/bootstrap_test.go:402, server_test.go:245) and the test makes dozens of such calls, so a sum of those contexts would mean nothing. The stopped windows, the commit waits and the quiet check lie inside renewal windows that are already counted, so they add nothing.

| Step | Minimum (s) | Typical (s) | Slowest passing, observed setup and tail (s) | Every wait at its limit (s) | Basis |
|------|-------------|-------------|----------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|-------|
| setup to `joinHostCert`'s return | 10 | 15 | 20 | 145 | observed: TestHostCertNoTOFU took 26.76 s to 29.41 s and TestHostCertIssueAndServe 30.00 s to 32.48 s in all, subtests included (the base logs). At the limit: the setup's seven 15 s waits and its 30 s loop of the census below (135 s), plus about 10 s of tool time |
| c1 | 1 | 5 | 60 | 60 | the first fetch poll after the first apply; bound 60 s |
| steps 1 to 3 inside c1's window, overlapped by the wait for r1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | they end by c1 plus about 20 s typically |
| r1 from c1 | 52 | 58 | 75 | 75 | RenewAt and the base readings |
| the one re-anchor (only when an anchor was late) | 0 | 0 | 75 | 75 | one more renewal |
| steps 4 to 6a inside r1's window | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | ceremony 2 and the switch take under a second; the commit and the quiet check end by r1 plus 50 s |
| r2 from r1 | 52 | 58 | 75 | 75 | as r1 |
| steps 7 (its keyscan) to 9 | 3 | 5 | 15 | 45 | five logins, a sync, an export and audit verify; at the limit the tail's two keyscans of the census (30 s) plus the tail's tool time as observed in the slowest-passing column (15 s), the same method as the setup row |
| cleanup | 0 | 0 | 0 | 40 | the cleanups of the eight daemons still running at the end, each waiting up to 5 s for its exit after SIGTERM (census below); the observed columns take the setup from whole-test readings, which include their own cleanups |
| **TestHostCARotation** | **about 118** | **about 141** | **about 320** | **about 515** | |

**The census of bounded waits, walked through the helpers' code from the first call to the last** (the plan check's third round, at 3d0d1b4; neither PR #115 nor the three changes merged after it, PR #114, PR #105 and PR #116, changed a file under test/, so it holds at c6c632d). The "every wait at its limit" figure is the sum of the waits listed here at their limits, plus the observed tool time of the setup (10 s) and of the tail (15 s); a wait that lies inside a window counted elsewhere adds nothing, because exceeding it fails that window's bound:
- **Setup** (`startServerEnv` with `StoppableSigner`, server_test.go:98-136, then the admin CLI and `joinHostCert`): `prepareSigner` (bootstrap_test.go:167-170) runs `initSigner`, whose role keys live in an ssh-agent, `bopts["socket"] = startAgent(t)` (:204), and `signGenesis`, with `e.AdminAgent = startAgent(t)` (:246) and the root's `rootSock = startAgent(t)` (:297); each `startAgent` ends in `waitFor(t, "ssh-agent socket", ...)` (harness_test.go:206), 15 s each (`waitFor`'s deadline, harness_test.go:161-174): 45 s. `serveStoppable`'s signer socket `waitFor` (rotation_test.go:211): 15 s. The server's `/healthz` `waitFor` (server_test.go:131): 15 s. The admin's `keyroster init` (`adminCLI`, approve_test.go:28-37) and the `kr` calls are tool calls. `joinHostCert` (hostcert_test.go:51-88): `newEnrolledHostLayout` (:59) starts the host sshd with `startIncludeSSHD` (agent_enroll_test.go:130), whose port `waitFor` (agent_apply_test.go:125) is 15 s; `install`, `identity` and `approve` are tool calls; the pending loop (hostcert_test.go:66) is 30 s; the first-apply `waitFor` (:83) is 15 s. In all 135 s. A wait tests its deadline only after its condition has returned (harness_test.go:161-174; the pending loop at hostcert_test.go:66 likewise), so a wait whose condition makes a timed call can overrun its limit by that call: `/healthz` by one HTTP request of the test's client, whose timeout is 10 s (server_test.go:178), the pending loop by one `kr` call (a 60 s context, server_test.go:245), the sshd port by one 200 ms dial (agent_apply_test.go:126); such an overrun is tool time, which the figure counts at its observed size, as it counts every tool call.
- **The certificates:** c1 within 60 s of `joinHostCert`'s return (`hcrFirst`), r1 by c1's issuance plus 75 s, the one re-anchor 75 s, r2 by r1's issuance plus 75 s (`hcrNext`'s `waitHostCerts`, hostcert_test.go:127-138). Step 0's keyscan and steps 1 to 3 (keyscans, a sync, logins, the two stopped windows with their `serveStoppable` waits and stop waits, rotation_test.go:211 and :216-221, and the bundle-2 commit wait of 45 s) lie inside r1's window or make r1 late, which the re-anchor covers; r1's own keyscan in `hcrNext` lies before its freshness check, inside the anchor rule; steps 4 to 6a (the switch's stopped window, serve's 10 s refusal context, the bundle-3 commit wait and the quiet check) lie inside r1's window, which ends by plus 50 s.
- **Tail:** r2's keyscan in `hcrNext` (`hcrServe`, 15 s) and step 8's keyscan that sshd still serves r2 (15 s); the logins, the sync, the copy, the export and audit verify are tool calls (runBin and env.keyroster carry 60 s contexts, bootstrap_test.go:402, server_test.go:245).
- **Cleanup:** `startDaemon` registers a cleanup that sends SIGTERM and waits up to 5 s (harness_test.go:145-151); eight daemons are still running when the test ends: the three ssh-agents (harness_test.go:205), the last signer serve (rotation_test.go:209; the three it replaced have exited), the server (server_test.go:130), the host sshd (agent_apply_test.go:123), the agent's run (agent_enroll_test.go:153) and its fetcher (:179): 40 s.
- **Outside the sum:** a `hostTrustKR` call that keeps meeting the rate-limit text (hosttrust_test.go:103-106), the exception below.

**The failing bound.** A failing run stops at its first failing assertion: at most the passing figure up to that point plus one tool timeout of 60 s, about 575 s at the limit, under `-timeout 20m` in both lanes. The one exception: a `hostTrustKR` call that keeps meeting the rate-limit text tries up to six times with 2 s sleeps (hosttrust_test.go:103-106), so it can add up to 370 s; `-timeout 20m` may then cut the lane, which fails either way.

## The budget (from the readings of both E2E runs of 3d0d1b4)

The readings are those of 3d0d1b4, the tree this plan was first planned on: the PR-head run 44861507363 (head 2d8f93d, whose tree equals 3d0d1b4's) and main's push run 44867318533 (3d0d1b4). This plan calls these two runs the base runs (their jobs the base jobs, run 44867318533 the base push run), their saved job logs the base logs and their figures the base readings: the names are from when the plan was written, and none of them is a run of c6c632d. Each figure below was read from the `ok ... test/e2e <s>` line of the job's real output in the saved job logs. The budget takes the larger reading of each lane. PR #115, which made 35d960b, changed no file under test/ or .github/ and only console texts and a doc comment in the code the lanes run, so these readings stand for 35d960b; Task 4's lane gate reads the PR's own lanes in any case. The base, c6c632d, is three changes after 35d960b, and the table's readings were not taken again: PR #114 changed action versions in the workflow files (no step, run line, matrix or timeout), PR #105 changed two modules that the lanes' binaries link, and PR #116 changed no file that the lanes compile. For comparison, the run of PR #105's rebased head (run 44970527778, head 1b59e8d, whose tree is the base's but for PR #116's six files) read, on the same `ok ... test/e2e <s>` lines, e2e-rotation 542.890 s (9.5p1) and 539.095 s (10.5p1), e2e 780.541 s (9.5p1) and 760.036 s (10.5p1), and e2e-probe 281.500 s (9.5p1) and 279.452 s (10.5p1): at most 8.137 s above the larger reading of its lane in the table (e2e-rotation 10.5p1), and below it in the main and probe lanes. The table and every figure derived from it keep the readings of 3d0d1b4.

| Lane | PR-head run | Main push run | Larger | With this plan |
|------|-------------|---------------|--------|----------------|
| e2e-rotation (9.5p1) | 530.481 s | 540.411 s | 540.411 s | typical about 681 s; slowest passing with observed setup and tail and the one re-anchor about 860 s; every wait of the census at its limit, with the observed tool time of the setup and the tail, about 1055 s |
| e2e-rotation (10.5p1) | 530.958 s | 527.927 s | 530.958 s | typical about 672 s; slowest passing as above about 851 s; every wait at its limit as above about 1046 s |
| e2e (9.5p1) | 774.194 s | 787.868 s | 787.868 s | unchanged: the file is not compiled there |
| e2e (10.5p1) | 798.716 s | 772.193 s | 798.716 s | unchanged |
| e2e-probe (9.5p1 / 10.5p1) | 292.476 / 280.247 s | 272.196 / 290.372 s | 292.476 / 290.372 s | unchanged |

In the rotation lanes TestCANextAndAbort took 35.07 s to 36.07 s and TestCARotationNoFailedLogin 468.14 s to 471.91 s (four jobs); 23 s to 36 s of each lane's `go test` lies outside the two tests (540.411 − 36.07 − 468.56 = 35.8 s in main's 9.5p1 job, 23 s to 25 s in the other three).

**The three figures per lane, each named for what it is:**
- **the typical run:** about 681 s (9.5p1) and 672 s (10.5p1);
- **the slowest passing run counted with the observed durations of the setup and the tail and the one re-anchor:** about 860 s and 851 s;
- **the sum of the bounded waits that the census of "The test's timing" lists, each at its limit (the setup's three ssh-agent sockets, signer socket, `/healthz`, sshd port, pending loop and first apply, c1, r1, the one re-anchor, r2, the tail's two keyscans and the cleanups), with the setup's and the tail's observed tool time:** about 1055 s and 1046 s (540.411 + 515 and 530.958 + 515). **This figure is above 900 s.** It is reached only when every wait of one run sits at its limit. The owner's rule (2026-11-17) is about the `go test` time a lane actually took in the PR's run: if either e2e-rotation lane's measured `go test` takes more than 900 s, the executor stops and reports the run; **no timeout is raised and no bound is tightened for it** (`-timeout 20m`, e2e.yml:170, and `timeout-minutes: 30`, :139, stay).

The failing bound is about 575 s at the limit (1115 s and 1106 s in the lanes), under `-timeout 20m`, except for a `hostTrustKR` call that keeps meeting the rate-limit text, which can add up to 370 s and fails either way. No figure here promises that a run stays under 900 s; the measured time of the PR's run is the check. The lane has room for this test but not for further long tests: a later plan that adds rotation tests needs a lane of its own (new required checks and a ruleset change, the owner's call; "Notes for the orchestrator's report to the owner").

**The main lanes are the hard constraint.** They run the whole `-tags e2e` suite with no `-run` filter (e2e.yml:65) and read up to 798.716 s in the base runs against the house gate's 900 s; a test of about 140 s there would pass 900 s. So the file's first line is exactly `//go:build e2e && e2erotation`, checked twice: by `head -1` and by `go list` (the new file is a test file of the package under `-tags "e2e e2erotation"` and under no other tag set the lanes use). Task 4's lane gate shows that neither main lane's real output names TestHostCARotation and prints both main lanes' `go test` times beside the base readings above; the house e2e gate holds them at or below 900 s. A main-lane reading above 900 s, with the file shown not to compile there, is reported as drift for the orchestrator, and no timeout is raised.

## What stays UNVERIFIED, in the words the documents use

> The test proves the gate's failure and success on one host and two clients on loopback, against OpenSSH 9.5p1 and 10.5p1 built by the lane. UNVERIFIED after it: the host drain's end on a real host and client (unit-tested only), a real fleet, a client on Windows, clients older than OpenSSH 8.5, real clients that never refresh, a `known-hosts sync` that an admin runs for another account, a host that has not taken the bundle naming the new host CA when it renews (unit-tested only, and no test shows that it keeps its certificate through that refusal), and a host certificate issued by hand with `keyroster ca issue --ca host` across a host-CA switch; 03-16 runs a real client.

The test does not wait for the gate's own time (the user profile's max TTL): it shows what the gate prevents, not the wait. No tool counts refreshed clients, so whether a fleet's clients have refreshed is the operator's to know.

ca-rotation.md, "The host CA" (:630-637) becomes, with the anchor that makes each sentence true:

> The same steps switch the host role, with one more gate: ceremony 2 for
> the host role waits until clients have refreshed. What differs for the
> host role is below.
>
> **Which clients.** The gate concerns every account whose ssh reads
> keyroster's managed trust files for the enrolled hosts. `keyroster login`
> and `keyroster known-hosts sync` write them into the `~/.ssh` of the
> account they run as, and ssh reads them through the
> `Include ~/.ssh/keyroster_ssh_config` line at top level of
> `~/.ssh/config`, before its first Host or Match line
> ([enroll-user.md](enroll-user.md#trusting-hosts-without-tofu), item 1).
> Such an account gains a host CA line only when it refreshes: a refresh
> writes one `@cert-authority` line for each host CA the trust bundle
> trusts at the client's clock, and narrowing never adds one. An account
> that never had these files keeps its own configuration: keyroster
> writes nothing that its ssh reads.
>
> **The gate.** Install ceremony 2 for the host role no earlier than
> ceremony 1's install plus the user profile's max TTL: the largest user
> `max_ttl_seconds` of the policy in force at ceremony 1's install and of
> every policy installed since (`--user-max-ttl` at genesis, 12 h by
> default). By the end of the wait every user certificate issued before
> ceremony 1's install under the policy then in force has expired; one
> issued before the install under an earlier policy with a larger user
> cap may still be valid; so a user whose only certificates were issued
> before that install under the policy then in force needs a new one,
> from a login or from an admin. The wait does not show that a login
> refreshed. Before ceremony 2's install, every account whose ssh reads
> the managed files has refreshed, a user who always passes
> `--no-known-hosts` included. An account has refreshed when its managed
> `~/.ssh/keyroster_known_hosts` holds the new host CA's line: the
> `@cert-authority` line that ends `keyroster host CA generation <g>`, g
> being the host generation that ceremony 1 added (its CA-change line
> `CA host gen <g> SHA256:...: none -> next`). The ways to refresh:
>
> - A user refreshes with a `keyroster login` without
>   `--no-known-hosts`, run as the account ssh runs as and on the computer
>   it runs from. The file shows whether it wrote the line: the login
>   succeeds whatever its host trust outcome, and one that ran with
>   `--no-known-hosts` adds no host CA line: it only narrows the file
>   (enroll-user.md, "Narrowing"). A user who always passes
>   `--no-known-hosts` runs one `keyroster login` without it.
> - An admin refreshes with `keyroster known-hosts sync`, which exits 0
>   when it refreshed; the file, not the exit, shows the new line. It
>   needs an admin key from the root-signed policy, loaded in ssh-agent.
> - An account that has the managed files and neither a login of its own
>   nor an admin key (an automation account, or a host that ssh connects
>   onward from) is refreshed only by an admin who runs
>   `keyroster known-hosts sync` as that account after ceremony 1's
>   install and before ceremony 2's install, and who then checks that
>   account's file for the line. If nobody does, it is a client that
>   never refreshes. (UNVERIFIED: no test runs sync for another account.)
>
> Whichever way, the line is the check. A login or a sync writes the
> host CAs of the trust bundle the server gave it when it started, and
> for a short time after ceremony 1's install the server can still give
> the earlier bundle, so a refresh that wrote only the old line is
> repeated until the file holds the new one.
>
> No tool counts the clients that have refreshed, and none holds the
> switch of the host role back: `root sign` and the install check a host
> switch as they check any other, so the gate is the operator's.
>
> **The hosts before the switch.** "Wait for the hosts" counts each
> host's `user_ca_keys` file, which a bundle that changes only the host
> role leaves as it is. For the host role read instead the line
> `bundle v<N> on <X>/<M> hosts` of `keyroster host status`: before
> ceremony 2 it reads M/M, with N the server's current bundle, ceremony
> 1's or a later one that still lists the new host CA (a bundle installed
> in between still lists it when its ceremony printed no `CA host` line
> in the CA-change section, as `root sign` and `keyroster trust verify`
> print it), and a host removed from the directory stays in M until
> `keyroster host forget`. A host that has not taken a bundle that
> names the new host CA refuses the certificate the signer issues it
> after the switch (`answer_unpinned_ca`; Run in CI: `TestVerifyAnswer`,
> its `unpinned_ca` case, and
> `TestHostCertStep/hostcert_answer_drained_ca_refused`, an answer by a
> host CA outside the agent's trusted set; UNVERIFIED end to end) and
> asks again after a back-off of 60 s up to an hour (Run in CI:
> `TestDecide/backoff_schedule`). It keeps the certificate it has
> (UNVERIFIED: no test shows a kept certificate surviving that refusal).
> So it stays on its old certificate until it takes that bundle or the
> old certificate runs out, after which clients refuse it.
>
> **The switch.** For the host role the floor of "Ceremony 2" uses the
> host profile's `max_ttl_seconds` (720 h by default), not the user one:
> the old host CA is trusted until the ceremony's issued_at plus the
> larger host `max_ttl_seconds` of the two policies, plus 300 s, plus the
> allowance.
>
> **What the hosts do.** After the switch each host whose certificate
> keyroster-agent renews keeps serving its certificate by the old host CA
> and moves to the new host CA when that certificate is next replaced:
> normally at its own next renewal, at two thirds of its certificate's
> lifetime, up to an hour early, unless the agent replaces it earlier
> because it is missing or near its end, the host's principals in the
> directory changed, its CA is no longer trusted, or the KRL revokes it.
> A certificate the agent obtains lives the smaller of 30 days and the
> host cap, so at the default cap of 720 h such a host renews about 20
> days after its own last certificate, host by host: at most about 20
> days after the switch, and for many hosts sooner. A host that
> keyroster-agent does not manage, whose certificate an admin issued with
> `keyroster ca issue --ca host`, keeps that certificate for as long as
> its `--ttl`, up to the host cap, and moves to the new host CA only when
> an admin issues it a new one after the switch (UNVERIFIED: no test
> issues a host certificate by hand across a host-CA switch).
>
> **What a client meets.** A client whose managed file holds the new host
> CA's line holds both host CA lines and accepts either certificate,
> before and after each host's move. A client whose managed file has not
> been refreshed since before ceremony 1's install holds only the old host
> CA's line: it reaches each host until that host moves, and refuses it
> with `Host key verification failed` from then on, possibly weeks after
> the switch, until it refreshes. Watching for failures right after the
> switch shows little, because most hosts move only at their renewals. A
> client whose managed `keyroster_known_hosts` is never rewritten by a
> full refresh cannot be rotated safely.
>
> **The drain.** No file that sshd reads lists a host CA key. The old host
> CA drains in the agents and on the clients: each agent stops accepting
> answers signed by it at its `trusted_until` (an agent's answer set is
> the host CAs the bundle trusts at the agent's clock), and every
> keyroster command that verifies the trust bundle removes its line from
> `keyroster_known_hosts` once its `trusted_until` has passed on the
> client's clock (enroll-user.md, "Narrowing"). For the host role the
> guarantee of "What this guarantees and what it does not" reads against
> the clients: every certificate the signer issued with the old host CA
> key and the verified log records expires at least 300 s before that
> key's `trusted_until`, the earliest moment a client stops trusting it.
>
> Run in CI: `TestHostCARotation` (the `e2e-rotation` job, OpenSSH 9.5p1
> and 10.5p1, one host at a host cap of 90 s and two clients on
> loopback). The test waits for the host's heartbeat to report ceremony
> 1's bundle before the switch. A client whose managed file holds both
> host CA lines after a sync the test checks line by line reaches the
> host by the old host CA's certificate right after the
> switch and by the new one's after the host's renewal; in between the
> host asks for no certificate, installs no other and its agent refuses
> no answer; a client that did not refresh reaches it
> right after the switch, is refused with `Host key verification failed`
> after the renewal, and reaches it again once its known_hosts file holds
> the lines a fresh refresh writes. The test does not wait the gate's
> time and does not wait for the host drain. UNVERIFIED: the host drain's
> end on a real host and client (unit-tested only), a real fleet, a
> client on Windows, clients older than OpenSSH 8.5, real clients that
> never refresh, and a sync run for another account.

ca-rotation.md, "Run in CI" (:681-697): `TestHostCARotation` joins the list after `TestCARotationNoFailedLogin`'s parenthesis, as "`TestHostCARotation` (the same job: what the host CA's gate prevents, a client that refreshed and one that did not across the host's renewal)"; the UNVERIFIED sentence reads "UNVERIFIED: the drain on real hosts of a fleet, the machine CA, the host drain's end on a real host and client (unit-tested only), every physical TPM, the homelab vTPM signer, every YubiKey, and every homelab signer; a real client run is 03-16's." The rest of the section stays.

ca-rotation.md, "What changes for nobody" (:739-745): at the end of the section, after the user-role sentence that ends "because the hosts trust the old key until its end.", the sentence "A switch of the host role needs no configuration change either, but a client must have refreshed first, as "The host CA" says." It stands after that sentence, not before it, so that the user-role sentence is not read as being about the host switch. The rest stays.

> **4a. A host-CA rotation (T-03-417).** A new host CA reaches a client
> only through a refresh: a `keyroster login` without `--no-known-hosts`
> or an admin's `keyroster known-hosts sync` writes its `@cert-authority`
> line (for the rotation's gate, an account has refreshed once its
> managed `keyroster_known_hosts` holds the new host CA's line, the one
> that ends `keyroster host CA generation <g>`), and narrowing never adds
> one. A refresh writes the host CAs of the trust bundle the server gave
> it when it started, which shortly after ceremony 1's install can still
> be the earlier one, so a refresh that wrote only the old line is
> repeated. Each host whose
> certificate keyroster-agent renews moves to the new host CA when its
> certificate is next replaced: at its own renewal, counted from its own
> last certificate, so at most about 20 days after the switch at the
> default host cap and for many hosts sooner, or earlier when its
> certificate is replaced for another reason; a host that keyroster-agent
> does not manage moves only when an admin issues it a new certificate
> with `keyroster ca issue --ca host`. A client whose managed
> `keyroster_known_hosts` has not been refreshed since the new host CA
> was added (ceremony 1 of the rotation) holds only the old host CA's
> line, so it refuses each host from that host's move on, with
> `Host key verification failed`, until it refreshes. A client whose
> managed file is never refreshed (an account with no login of its own
> and no admin key, such as automation that only runs `ssh`) cannot be
> rotated safely, unless an admin runs `keyroster known-hosts sync` as
> that account after ceremony 1's install and before ceremony 2's
> install and the file then holds the new host CA's line

> e2e: `TestHostCARotation` (OpenSSH 9.5p1 and 10.5p1, one host and two
> clients on loopback). UNVERIFIED on a real client and on Windows, and

## The lane gate and its proof (carry-in item 1)

The gate reads the PR's pull_request E2E run for HEAD with `scripts/gh-as-bot.sh run view <id> --log` (one line per log line: job, step, then the timestamp and the text, tab-separated) and drops every echoed step script first, with the filter of 03-12f's deviation 21: `awk '/Z ##\[group\]Run /{g=1} { if(!g) print } /Z ##\[endgroup\]/{g=0}'` (a `##[group]Runner ...` line of the set-up step does not match `Run ` with its space). Then, for each of `e2e-rotation (9.5p1)` and `e2e-rotation (10.5p1)`, on that job's lines only (`awk -F'\t' '$1==name'`):

| Leg | What it requires | On the base logs |
|-----|------------------|------------------|
| L1 | the job has lines | pass (372/372 and 351/350 lines) |
| L2 | exactly one `Z --- PASS: <name> (` line for each of TestCANextAndAbort, TestCARotationNoFailedLogin and TestHostCARotation (the `Z ` of the timestamp makes it column 0 of the test output, so a subtest's indented line never counts) | fails on TestHostCARotation only (0 lines); the other two read 1 each |
| L3 | no line matching `Z +--- (SKIP\|FAIL): ` (top-level or subtest) | pass (0) |
| L4 | exactly one line holding the fixed line `host CA rotation: the refreshed client accepted generation 1 and generation 2, the client that did not refresh was refused` | fails (0) |
| L5 | exactly one line matching `_test\.go:[0-9]+: login failures: 0$`: TestCARotationNoFailedLogin's last log line (ca_rotation_test.go:879); its step lines end in `, login failures: 0` and do not match | pass (1 each) |
| L6 | exactly one `ok ... github.com/Labontese/keyroster/test/e2e <s>s` line, with s above 0 and at most 900 | pass: 530.481 and 530.958 (PR head), 540.411 and 527.927 (main) |

The PR gate also requires both rotation jobs' and both main e2e jobs' conclusions to be `success` (from the run's JSON, not the log), and, for `e2e (9.5p1)` and `e2e (10.5p1)`, that no line of the job's real output names TestHostCARotation (0 in the base logs; the main lanes' step scripts never name it), printing each main lane's `go test` time beside the base readings.

**Negative controls, run while this plan was written on copies of both saved base logs.** Because the base logs lack TestHostCARotation, the controls were run on a synthetic positive: each base log with three lines added after each rotation lane's `--- PASS: TestCARotationNoFailedLogin (` line (`=== RUN   TestHostCARotation`, the fixed line as a `hostca_rotation_test.go:300:` log line, and `--- PASS: TestHostCARotation (150.00s)`, with the same job, step and timestamp). Results, the same on both logs:

| Control | Result |
|---------|--------|
| the synthetic positive | exit 0, `e2e-rotation (9.5p1): go test 530.481 s` and `(10.5p1) ... 530.958 s` (PR head), 540.411 s and 527.927 s (main) |
| N1: TestHostCARotation's PASS line removed in 10.5p1 | exit 1, `10.5p1: not exactly one PASS line for TestHostCARotation` |
| N2: `--- SKIP: TestHostCARotation (0.00s)` added; N2b: an indented subtest SKIP added | exit 1, `9.5p1: a SKIP or FAIL result` (both) |
| N3: the fixed line removed in 9.5p1 | exit 1, `9.5p1: not exactly one fixed line` |
| N4: `login failures: 0` changed to `login failures: 1` | exit 1, `9.5p1: not exactly one final login failures: 0` |
| N5: the 10.5p1 `go test` time set to 900.001 s | exit 1, `10.5p1: go test time [900.001] missing, repeated or above 900 s`; at 900.000 s in both lanes the gate passes (the owner's rule stops above 900 s) |
| N5c: the 9.5p1 `ok` line removed | exit 1, `9.5p1: go test time [] missing, ...` |
| N6: the PASS line removed from the real output and placed inside the echoed group of the PASS-loop step | exit 1, `9.5p1: not exactly one PASS line for TestHostCARotation` (the raw log then holds 2 such lines; the filter drops the echoed one) |
| N7: the fixed line only inside an echoed group | exit 1, `9.5p1: not exactly one fixed line` |
| N8: the 10.5p1 rotation job's lines removed | exit 1, `10.5p1: no lines` |
| N9: a top-level `--- FAIL:` line added | exit 1, `10.5p1: a SKIP or FAIL result` |
| N10: a second final `login failures: 0` line | exit 1, `9.5p1: not exactly one final login failures: 0` |

**Which of the gate's strings stand in a step script after this plan's edit.** The echoed scripts of the rotation job hold the `-run` list and the PASS loop, so they name `TestHostCARotation`, but as `|TestHostCARotation)$'` and `for t in ... TestHostCARotation; do grep -q -- "^--- PASS: $t ("`: no echoed line holds `--- PASS: TestHostCARotation (`, the fixed line, `login failures` or an `ok` line (0 such lines in the echoed groups of both base logs, and this plan adds none: no step greps the fixed line). The filter removes them anyway. Inside CI each check reads the lane's own `tee` file, which holds test output only.

## Budget

files_modified lists 13 files: 4 test and workflow files (the new test, ca_rotation_test.go, e2e.yml, CONTRIBUTING.md), 4 runbooks (ca-rotation.md, enroll-user.md, machine-identities.md, signer-install.md), 2 runbook test files and 3 planning files (this plan, ROADMAP, STATE). The SUMMARY is the one file the branch adds beyond the list.

Per task (tokens raw, by the method of 03-12f-PLAN.md's Budget: each task session reads the shared sections of this plan, about 30k tokens, plus its own task, the code it reads and its runs): Task 1 35k (the checks, the branch, the base measurement); Task 2 180k (a test of about 400 lines with the quiet check and its attempts assertion and the re-anchor rule, and the helper change, the wrapper and its self-check, the local pre-check on both OpenSSH versions of about 14 minutes, three e2e mutant runs of about 3 minutes each and one unit run, the PASS-loop proof, lint under four configurations); Task 3 175k (two test files, four runbooks with every sentence checked against its anchor, the RED from a worktree, 70 document mutants of a few seconds each, each with its record); Task 4 150k (the plan's own staged diff, about 55k tokens, the delta and full gates, the PR and its CI wait of about 14 minutes per round, the lane gate, the tracking update and the SUMMARY); Task 5 5k. **That is about 545k raw at low confidence** (no calibration samples, so the projection is not scaled; comparable plans went 2x to 5x higher, 03-12f's realized 198k against 870k planned being the exception). It is above the specification's 250k because the specification did not count the local pre-check and the mutant runs that carry-in items 7 and 8 add, nor the two runbooks and the pins that the plan check's sweep added. It is one PR in the specification's scope: nothing is cut.

**Test time (Q-09-3, owner decision, 03-09-PLAN.md).** Only cmd/keyroster changes in a way that costs test time (two runbook test files: one new leaf, one changed leaf, one new check line). Last readings: 03-12f's base `keyroster=306.247` with 281.186 and 316.143 in its other sessions (03-12f-SUMMARY.md:73-77). These orient; the gate is the delta over the base this plan measures.
- **Timeout.** Every go test command carries `-timeout 15m` (the e2e commands `-timeout 20m`, as the lane), every mutant command `-timeout 5m`, except the local end-to-end control and M371 to M373, which carry `-timeout 12m` (the mutant rule, "Delivery protocol"). A timeout is not a kill.
- **Base.** Task 1 measures a quiet base on the unchanged tree right after its tree check, with no other heavy job running: `bash scripts/linux.sh 'exec 2>&1; go test -race -count=1 -timeout 15m ./cmd/keyroster/'`, in the background. It records exactly one SUMMARY line, `test-time base: keyroster=<s> at=<epoch seconds>`, the time read from the package's `ok` line without the trailing `s`.
- **The rule.** Task 4's delta gate runs the same command on the branch and stops if the package adds more than 90 s to its base. A base more than 24 h old, or measured in another session or WSL boot, is re-measured on origin/main in a detached worktree outside the repository, immediately before; a re-measure replaces the base line in place. Expected: +0 to +3 s; a reading above +60 s is a warning recorded with the cases that cost most.
- **Whole module.** `go test -race -count=1 -timeout 15m ./...` is pass or fail only.
- **The CI lanes this plan changes.** The e2e-rotation lanes only, as "The budget" says; the owner's 900 s rule binds them, and Task 4's lane gate reads it.
- **Ruleset.** 30 required checks (`.github/rulesets/main-integrity.json`, CONTRIBUTING.md:63); this plan adds no job and renames none, so scripts/apply-rulesets.sh is not needed and is not run.

## Phase Goal

> Enrolled Linux hosts are kept in sync automatically (trusted CAs, principals, KRL and host certificates), so access granted, revoked or rotated centrally takes effect across the fleet without hand-edited sshd config and without risking lockout. (ROADMAP Goal, verbatim.)
