**One commit, G2** (`test(e2e): rotate the host CA end to end with a refreshed and a stale client`), after the local pre-check below has passed on both OpenSSH versions. There is no RED for a test that only a lane with OpenSSH builds runs; the proof is the pre-check, the mutants and Task 4's lane gate.

**test/e2e/ca_rotation_test.go: the two helpers by role** (per the specification's scope; byte-identical behaviour for TestCARotationNoFailedLogin):
- `caRotDrainRE` (:526-527) becomes `func caRotDrainRE(role string) *regexp.Regexp`, returning the same pattern with `user` replaced by `regexp.QuoteMeta(role)`; its comment says it is install-bundle's drain line for the role's generation 1 (03-12e, 03-12f2). The one call (:787) becomes `caRotDrainRE("user").FindStringSubmatch(out)`.
- `caRotRetireKey(t, sg, fp1)` (:548) becomes `caRotRetireKey(t *testing.T, sg *signerEnv, role, fp1 string) string`: `"--role", role` in both runs (:550, :599), `head := "role " + role + " generation 1 " + fp1 + ": "` (:554), the file names `"retire-" + role + "-g1"` and `"retire-" + role + "-g1-cert.pub"` in the two command prefixes and the two cases (:555-556, :566, :569), and the second run's expected line `"retire-key: no draining key of role " + role + " is held"` (:600); its comment names the role. Every other line, the unknown-line refusal (:573-575), the socket and count checks (:580-582, :595-597) and the `t.Logf` line (:598) included, stays as it is. The one call (:806) becomes `caRotRetireKey(t, sg, "user", fp1)`. No other line of the file changes (the first verify command holds the diff to these lines).

**test/e2e/hostca_rotation_test.go (new), first line exactly `//go:build e2e && e2erotation`**, package e2e, with TestHostCARotation as "The fixed test, and what it must assert" specifies, in that order of steps, and these declarations of its own (names free at the base):
- constants with comments that state their anchors: `hcrCap = 90 * time.Second` (the host cap); `hcrRenewMin = 51 * time.Second` and `hcrRenewMax = 75 * time.Second` (a renewal's issuance after the previous issuance: RenewAt is at least the previous issuance plus 52 s, hostcert.go:556, :549, :564-568, and the house bound keeps a second of slack, hostcert_test.go:408; the agent counts a certificate missing 15 s before its ValidBefore, hostcert.go:546); `hcrC1StopBy = 40 * time.Second` and `hcrC1UpBy = 50 * time.Second` (c1's stopped windows: each starts by c1's issuance plus 40 s and the signer serves again by plus 50 s, before the earliest renewal request at plus 52 s; a request that meets a stopped signer costs a 60 s back-off, hostcert.go:64 and :593, longer than what is left of a 90 s certificate); r1's window, every bound before plus 52 s, derived as "The test's timing" (Back-off) says: `hcrSwitchStart = 19 * time.Second` (the switch starts by then: 4 s after the latest fresh observation of r1 for ceremony 2, which took about 23 ms in the base runs), `hcrSwitchUp = 23 * time.Second` (serving again: 8 s after the latest fresh observation, 4 s for a stopped window that took under 0.1 s in the base runs), `hcrStep6 = 28 * time.Second` (step 6 done), `hcrCommit3 = 44 * time.Second` (the bundle-3 commit seen: 21 s after the latest serve, for the path of about 20 s: the server's 5 s status cache, a fetch poll of at most 5.83 s, the 1 s applier round, the next poll's heartbeat within 5.83 s and the test's 2 s poll), `hcrQuiet = 45 * time.Second` (the quiet check starts, at least 1 s after the commit bound) and `hcrQuietEnd = 50 * time.Second` (the quiet check's keyscan ends by then; each try's context is bounded by the time left, so the check ends by plus 50 s); `hcrFresh = 15 * time.Second` (an anchor certificate observed later than this after its issuance is replaced by its renewal, once per test); `hcrCommit = 45 * time.Second` (the bundle-2 commit after its install; the fetcher sends a heartbeat each 5 s poll); `hcrFirst = 60 * time.Second` (the first certificate after `joinHostCert` returns); `hcrServe = 15 * time.Second` (sshd serving an installed certificate); `hcrAllowance = 60 * time.Second` (the switch's `--drain-allowance`, never 0: the agent asks for certificates by itself); `hcrDrain = hcrCap + 300*time.Second + hcrAllowance` (bundle 3's trusted_until after its issued_at: castate.go:122, :134-150); and `hcrLine`, the fixed line of step 9, verbatim.
- `hcrIssued(c *ssh.Certificate) time.Time`: `time.Unix(int64(c.ValidAfter)+300, 0)`, the issuance second (`hostcert.IssuedAt`, hostcert.go:542, `Backdate = 300` :55).
- `hcrServes(t, h *agentHost, want *ssh.Certificate, within time.Duration)`: runs `ssh-keyscan -c -t ed25519 -p <port> 127.0.0.1` from the OpenSSH prefix, every 500 ms, each try with a context of the smaller of 5 s and the time left until `within` ends (no try starts after it has ended), until it exits 0 and prints a certificate (the line read with and without its first field, as hostcert_test.go:160-171 does) whose `Marshal()` equals `want.Marshal()`; it fails with the serials it saw when `within` passes. So the helper returns by the end of `within` plus the exit of one ssh-keyscan whose context has ended.
- `hcrNext(t, eh *enrolledHost, prev *ssh.Certificate, ca ssh.PublicKey, what string) *ssh.Certificate`: waits with `waitHostCerts` for one certificate whose serial is above `prev`'s, with the deadline `hcrIssued(prev).Add(hcrRenewMax)`; a remaining time at or below zero is passed as zero, and `waitHostCerts` still looks once before it tests its deadline (hostcert_test.go:129-136), so a late call is never failed for its own lateness (its message: `<what>: no renewal installed by <prev>'s issuance plus 75 s`); it notes the time the call started, and requires `hcrIssued(next) - hcrIssued(prev)` in `[hcrRenewMin, hcrRenewMax)`: when the gap is 75 s or more and the call started after `hcrIssued(prev).Add(hcrRenewMax)`, the message names the late call (`<what>: looked at <start>, after <prev>'s issuance plus 75 s, and found a renewal issued <d> s after it: the step before this call ran long`), and otherwise it names the agent's schedule (`<what>: the renewal was issued <d> s after the previous issuance, want in [51, 75): renew at issued + 60 - j, j < 9`); the `SignatureKey` equal to `ca` (message names both fingerprints), `hostCertLifetime` 90, and `hcrServes`; it logs the serials and the gap.
- `hcrAnchor(t, eh, c, prev *ssh.Certificate, ca, what, reanchored *bool) (anchor, renewed *ssh.Certificate)`, with `prev` the certificate `c` renewed (nil for the first certificate): returns `(c, prev)` when `time.Since(hcrIssued(c)) <= hcrFresh`; otherwise, if the test has not re-anchored yet (`*reanchored` false), it sets the flag, logs that the anchor was late, and returns `(hcrNext(t, eh, c, ca, what+" (re-anchored)"), c)`, the late certificate's renewal and the late certificate itself, the renewal having to be fresh itself; a second late anchor, of either certificate, fails the test (`<what>: observed <d> after its issuance, and the test's one re-anchor is spent`). One flag for the whole test, shared by c1's and r1's calls.
- `hcrQuietCheck(t, eh, prev, r1)` (`prev` is the certificate r1 renewed): sleeps until `hcrIssued(r1).Add(hcrQuiet)`; fails if that moment has already passed (`the quiet check starts after r1 + 45 s`); then, in this order: (1) `hcrAttempts(t, eh, prev, r1)`; (2) `installedHostCerts` names exactly one certificate, r1 by serial (message names the serials); (3) `hcrServes(t, h, r1, time.Until(hcrIssued(r1).Add(hcrQuietEnd)))`; (4) `hcrNoRefusal(t, eh)`.
- `hcrAttempts(t, eh, prev, r1)`: reads `filepath.Join(eh.state, hostcert.AttemptsFile)` with `os.ReadFile` and parses it with `hostcert.ParseAttempts` (package `github.com/Labontese/keyroster/internal/agent/hostcert`; test/e2e already imports internal packages, for example internal/agent/heartbeat); with `r := uint64(hcrIssued(r1).Unix())` and `p := uint64(hcrIssued(prev).Unix())` it requires that no key's `Requests` holds a `CreatedAt` above `r` (message: `step 6a: the host asked for a certificate at <CreatedAt as a time>, after r1's issuance <r>: <the file>`), that every key has `Failures == 0` and `LastCode == ""` (message names the key, the count and the code), and, as the positive control, that some key holds a request with `p < CreatedAt <= r` (r1's own request; message: `step 6a: the attempts file holds no request for r1 (created after <p>, by <r>): <the file>`). It is M373's named kill.
- `hcrNoRefusal(t, eh)`: requires that `eh.run.String()` holds no line containing `WARNING host certificate answer for` (the agent's refusal line, internal/agent/apply/hostcert.go:603); the message prints the lines found. It is the quiet check's second view of an early request (one the signer answered with r1 inside its recent window) and runs again after r2 for the whole test.
- `hcrStopped(t, env *serverEnv, what string, startBy, upBy time.Time, fn func() time.Time) (installed, servedAgain time.Time)`: fails when `time.Now()` is after `startBy` (`<what>: the signer stops at <t>, after <startBy>`), stops the signer (`env.StopSigner()`), runs `fn`, serves again (`env.StopSigner = serveStoppable(t, env.Signer)`), and fails when the serve returned after `upBy` (`<what>: the signer served again at <t>, after <upBy>, so a renewal request may have met a stopped signer`); it logs how long the signer was down and returns, in this order, the time `fn` hands back and the moment the signer served again, for `hcrCommitted`'s lags. `fn` returns `time.Now()` taken right after its install-bundle call returned: for ceremony 1's install that is the end of `fn`; for the switch it is taken before serve's refusal, retire-key, the agent listing and doctor, which run after the install inside the same stopped window, so the bundle-3 lag from the install is measured from the end of install-bundle itself. The prepare window's `fn` has no install and returns its own end, which no lag uses.
- `hcrSync(t, kr func(...string) string, khPath string, want ...ssh.PublicKey)`: `kr("known-hosts", "sync")` must print `Host trust refreshed (directory v`; `caLines(t, khPath)` must be exactly `len(want)` lines, each with the host pattern `web1` only, whose keys equal `want` as a set (byte comparison of `Marshal()`); the output is logged.
- `hcrLogin(t, cfg, login, src string, c agentCert, ca ssh.PublicKey, what string) (int, string)`: `trustedHostLogin(t, cfg, login, "web1", src, keyAuth(c), "-o", "HostKeyAlgorithms="+trustCertAlgs)`; it returns the exit code and output and logs the output's `Server host certificate` line. `hcrAccepted(...)` requires exit 0, a `Server host certificate` line that contains `ssh.FingerprintSHA256(ca)` (message: `<what>: ssh to web1 exited <n>, want 0 with Server host certificate by <fp>`), and `wantNoPrompt`. `hcrRefused(...)` requires a non-zero exit, `Host key verification failed`, the `Server host certificate` line naming `ca`'s fingerprint, and no TOFU text (message: `<what>: ssh to web1 exited <n>, want a refusal with Host key verification failed`).
- `hcrCommitted(t, eh, kr, version uint64, installed, servedAgain, by time.Time, what string)`: polls `hostsStatusJSON(t, kr)` every 2 s until `a.Current.Bundle == version` and the one host's heartbeat reports `Status.Committed(a.Current.Docs())` (as ca_rotation_test.go:829-831 does), and fails with the status JSON and the agent's run and fetch logs at `by`; an observation returned after `by` fails too, even when it saw the commit (message: `<what>: committed only at <t>, after <by>`), so in both uses the commit counts only when it was seen by its bound, and the quiet check at r1 plus 45 s starts at least 1 s after a commit seen by r1 plus 44 s. A correct product can fail this way only when a `host status` call is slow at the deadline. From `installed` and `servedAgain` (the two times `hcrStopped` returned for that bundle's install, in that order: the end of the install-bundle call, then the moment the signer served again) it logs both lags to the commit being seen, `bundle <n> committed <a> s after the install and <b> s after the signer served again`, for bundle 2 and bundle 3.
- The setup and the nine steps as specified, with these values: `home := requireRealHome(t)` and `sshDir := filepath.Join(home, ".ssh")` before `withGenesisHostMaxTTL(t, hcrCap)` and `startServerEnv`; `sg := env.Signer`; `gen1 := pubKey(t, readPub(t, sg.HostCAPub))` and `fp1 := ssh.FingerprintSHA256(gen1)`; the root as TestCARotationNoFailedLogin reads it (`sg.RootFingerprints[0]`, `filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(sg.BundleDir), "roots.pub")`, `sg.RootAgent`, ca_rotation_test.go:676); generation 2 is an Ed25519 key made with `sshKeygen` and added to `sg.RoleAgent` (`sshAdd`), `gen2` its public key and `fp2` its fingerprint; c1 is `c1, _ = hcrAnchor(t, eh, waitHostCerts(t, eh, 1, hcrFirst, ...)[0], nil, gen1, "c1", &reanchored)`, the test's one `reanchored` flag (c1 is then the first certificate, or its renewal after a re-anchor on it); prepare and the install of ceremony 1 run through `hcrStopped` with `startBy = hcrIssued(c1).Add(hcrC1StopBy)` and `upBy = hcrIssued(c1).Add(hcrC1UpBy)`; ceremony 1 runs while the signer serves; the bundle-2 commit is bounded by the install's end plus `hcrCommit`; `r1, r1prev := hcrAnchor(t, eh, hcrNext(t, eh, c1, gen1, "r1"), c1, gen1, "r1", &reanchored)`, so `r1prev` is the certificate r1 renewed in all three cases: c1 when nothing was late, c1's renewal after a re-anchor on c1 (then c1 holds it), and the late r1 itself after a re-anchor on r1; ceremony 2 follows at once; the switch runs through `hcrStopped` with `startBy` the earlier of `hcrIssued(r1).Add(hcrSwitchStart)` and bundle 3's issued_at plus 50 s, and `upBy = hcrIssued(r1).Add(hcrSwitchUp)`; inside it, install-bundle with `--previous` runs through `sg.signerCmd` and its drain line is parsed with `caRotDrainRE("host").FindStringSubmatch(out)` (the two times compared as step 5 says, both parsed with `trust.TimeFormat`, v against `time.Unix(int64(r1.ValidBefore), 0).UTC().Format(trust.TimeFormat)`), then serve is started once as ca_rotation_test.go:797-805 does (a 10 s context, which must not be what ended it), `caRotRetireKey(t, sg, "host", fp1)`, `caRotAgentHolds(t, sock, fp1)`, and doctor (`sg.signerCmd(t, "doctor", "--state-dir", sg.StateDir)`) with exit 0, the line `OK switch_floor: host generation 2: the log records no certificate of this key; the earliest is the floor` and no `draining_key_still_usable`; step 6's checks must end by `hcrIssued(r1).Add(hcrStep6)` (message: `step 6 ended at <t>, after r1 + 28 s`); the bundle-3 commit is waited for after step 6, with `by = hcrIssued(r1).Add(hcrCommit3)` (message: `bundle 3 not committed by r1 + 44 s, which the quiet check at r1 + 45 s needs`); then `hcrQuietCheck(t, eh, r1prev, r1)` (step 6a), whose positive control therefore looks for a request created after the issuance of the very certificate r1 renewed; r2 is `hcrNext(t, eh, r1, gen2, "r2")`; `hcrNoRefusal(t, eh)` runs right after r2 is observed (step 8); B's refusal comes from `net.IPv4(127, 0, 0, 2).String()`; the remedy copies the live `keyroster_known_hosts` over B's file with `copyFile(..., 0o600)` after a fresh `hcrSync` with both keys.
- Comments say what each step proves and which bound it uses, and cite `(03-12f2)`; a comment states only what the code path shows. The test has no goroutine and calls no helper that could call `t.Fatalf` off the test's goroutine. Its strings and log messages do not hold the word `go` followed by a space and a letter, an underscore or a parenthesis (the first verify command's goroutine leg reads string text too and would stop the task; it fails safe).

**.github/workflows/e2e.yml** (03-12e2's normalised form): the comment above the job (:132-135) becomes the four lines `  # The CA rotation lane (03-12b): the tests under the build tags e2e and`, `  # e2erotation, which the main e2e lanes do not compile. It runs`, `  # TestCANextAndAbort, TestCARotationNoFailedLogin (03-12e2) and`, `  # TestHostCARotation (03-12f2), each with a PASS check below.`; the `-run` list (:170) becomes `'^(TestCANextAndAbort|TestCARotationNoFailedLogin|TestHostCARotation)$'`; the PASS loop (:173) reads `for t in TestCANextAndAbort TestCARotationNoFailedLogin TestHostCARotation; do`. Nothing else changes: the job name, the matrix, `-timeout 20m` and `timeout-minutes: 30` stay, so no required check changes.

**CONTRIBUTING.md** :82-83, the two `e2e-rotation` rows only: :82 names the three tests, `(\`TestCANextAndAbort\`, \`TestCARotationNoFailedLogin\` and \`TestHostCARotation\`, build tags \`e2e e2erotation\`)`, and after "...with a certificate login every second that never fails;" adds "the third switches the host CA and shows that a client that refreshed keeps reaching the host across the host's renewal while a client that did not is refused after it;" before "the main `e2e` lanes do not run them"; :83 reads `| \`e2e-rotation (10.5p1)\` | \`e2e.yml\` | the same three tests against the OpenSSH 10.5p1 build |`. The sentence `requires these 30 checks` stays.

**Local checks before the commit**, in order (each exit 0):
1. The third verify command (vet under every e2e tag set, gofmt, golangci-lint for linux, windows, darwin and the e2e rotation tags on the new lines, the pinned actions, and the `go list` tag check).
2. **The namespace wrapper** (carry-in item 7). The executor writes a small script outside the repository (never committed, never under the repository's directory) that runs its arguments as a command inside an unprivileged user and mount namespace in which: `/etc/hosts` is a private copy of WSL's with `127.0.0.1 keyroster.localhost` added (WSL's DNS does not answer the name, so every server-backed e2e test fails at `keyroster init` without it; 03-12e2-SUMMARY.md, "Local pre-check before the push"); `$HOME/.ssh` is a private empty directory mounted over the real one, so the test's real-home writes (`requireRealHome`, hosttrust_test.go:57-93, writes the three managed files there and removes them at cleanup) never reach the owner's `~/.ssh`; the uid and gid are the caller's own (mapped back, so the account lookup and `$HOME` still agree, hosttrust_test.go:65-77); `KEYROSTER_E2E_REAL_HOME=1` is set and `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is unset (:59-60). It changes no system file. The executor never lists, reads or writes the real `~/.ssh` outside the namespace; if `$HOME/.ssh` is not a directory there to mount over, it stops and reports. Self-check, through scripts/linux.sh with the wrapper's absolute WSL path for `W`: `W=<the wrapper>; "$W" getent hosts keyroster.localhost | grep -q "^127\.0\.0\.1[[:space:]]" && test "$("$W" id -u)" = "$(id -u)" && "$W" mountpoint -q "$HOME/.ssh" && test -z "$("$W" find "$HOME/.ssh" -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 1 -print -quit)" && test "$("$W" printenv KEYROSTER_E2E_REAL_HOME)" = 1 && ! "$W" printenv GITHUB_ACTIONS >/dev/null && ! grep -q "keyroster\.localhost" /etc/hosts` must exit 0 (the `find` reads only the mounted empty directory, after the mount check).
3. **The local pre-check on both OpenSSH versions** (not the proof; the CI lane is), in the background with nothing else running in WSL, output into a directory `O` outside the repository: `bash scripts/linux.sh 'exec 2>&1; W=<the wrapper>; O=<the output directory>; "$W" env KEYROSTER_OPENSSH_PREFIX="$HOME/.cache/keyroster/openssh-10.5p1" go test -tags "e2e e2erotation" -run "^TestHostCARotation$" -count=1 -timeout 20m -v ./test/e2e/... > "$O/hostca-10.5p1.log" 2>&1; echo "exit $?" >> "$O/hostca-10.5p1.log"; "$W" env KEYROSTER_OPENSSH_PREFIX="$HOME/.cache/keyroster/openssh-9.5p1" go test -tags "e2e e2erotation" -run "^(TestCANextAndAbort|TestCARotationNoFailedLogin|TestHostCARotation)$" -count=1 -timeout 20m -v ./test/e2e/... > "$O/rotation-9.5p1.log" 2>&1; echo "exit $?" >> "$O/rotation-9.5p1.log"'` (about 14 minutes: the 9.5p1 run is the lane's own `-run` list, which also exercises the helper change in TestCARotationNoFailedLogin). It passes when the fourth verify command exits 0 on `O`. Before that, read both logs' `Server host certificate` lines: if either build's line does not name the signing CA's `SHA256:` fingerprint, stop and report ("Planner decisions"). Two more stops, with the lines as printed and no change to the test: an unmutated run whose agent log holds a `WARNING host certificate answer for` line (the whole-test assertion of `hcrNoRefusal` met a refusal no mutant caused, for example during the setup), and a login or keyscan from 127.0.0.1 that fails while sshd's log names a per-source penalty (OpenSSH 10.x PerSourcePenalties, reached by the repeated keyscans that share 127.0.0.1 with A's and B's positive logins). Neither assertion is narrowed and no source address is changed without the orchestrator's ruling. A first failure that names `clock_regression` or reads an age below its fixture's is re-run once unchanged ("One rule for clock flakes"); if the test fails on a build for any other reason, fix the test (never a bound or its anchor, never a timeout) and run both again; record every failed run with its cause in the SUMMARY.
4. Read `git diff --cached` of all four files in full, one file at a time; commit G2 signed, authored as Daniel.

**After G2, the mutants** (under the mutant rule; one exact replacement in the working tree at G2, restored and hash-checked after each; e2e mutants on 10.5p1 through the wrapper, each preceded by `go vet -tags "e2e e2erotation" ./test/e2e/` or, for M373, `go vet ./internal/agent/apply/`, and the unmutated control first: `bash scripts/linux.sh 'exec 2>&1; W=<the wrapper>; "$W" env KEYROSTER_OPENSSH_PREFIX="$HOME/.cache/keyroster/openssh-10.5p1" go test -tags "e2e e2erotation" -run "^TestHostCARotation$" -count=1 -timeout 12m -v ./test/e2e/...'` passing with its fixed line). Each e2e mutant runs the same command, with `-timeout 12m` (the mutant rule's form for these three: the whole test runs; its passing run with every wait at its limit is about 515 s and a failing one about 575 s, so 12 minutes keeps about 145 s above the failing figure); a kill comes at about 110 s (M373, at r1 plus 45 s) or 120 s (M371 and M372, at step 8) at the earliest and by about 320 s in a run whose waits keep their observed sizes, and a timeout is not a kill:
- **M371** (setup): step 1's copy for client B stays, and one more `copyFile` of the live `keyroster_known_hosts` over B's file is added right after step 3's sync check (B refreshed after all), so B passes steps 1, 3 and 6 as before. Expected: `--- FAIL: TestHostCARotation` with step 8's `client B after the renewal: ssh to web1 exited 0, want a refusal with Host key verification failed`; every step before it passes.
- **M372** (setup): right after step 3's sync check and logins, the live `~/.ssh/keyroster_known_hosts` (inside the namespace) is rewritten without the line that holds generation 2's key (A lacks the generation-2 line). Expected: `--- FAIL: TestHostCARotation` with step 8's `client A after the renewal: ssh to web1 exited 255, want 0 with Server host certificate by <fp2>`; step 6's logins pass (they meet generation 1).
- **M373** (product, the agent): internal/agent/apply/hostcert.go:414-418, `if ca.State == "draining" { continue }` as the first statement of the loop body (a certificate of a draining host CA counts as untrusted, so the host asks for a new certificate as soon as it applies bundle 3). Expected: `--- FAIL: TestHostCARotation` at step 6a's attempts assertion, `hcrAttempts`'s message `step 6a: the host asked for a certificate at <t>, after r1's issuance <r>`: the mutated agent makes its request in the applier pass that applied bundle 3, saves it in the attempts file before it sends it (apply/hostcert.go:925-926), and that pass wrote the committed status the test waited for, at least 1 s before the check (run.go:322-327). The assertion does not depend on what the signer answered. That answer decides only what the later checks would also have seen, had the test gone on: inside the signer's recent window (a third of the 90 s cap after r1's issuance, to r1 plus 30 s, internal/cert/hostrecent.go:62-64) the signer answers with r1 itself (internal/signer/hostissue.go:162-170), which the agent refuses and logs (hostcert.go:699-700; apply/hostcert.go:603), the log assertion's case; after the window it issues a new certificate, the drop-in check's case. Step 6 is not a firing point: it ends by r1 plus 28 s, inside the recent window, where the signer only re-delivers r1, which nothing installs. Step 7's gap would be the last one. The record names the assertion that fired and its message. Also run `bash scripts/linux.sh 'exec 2>&1; go vet ./internal/agent/apply/ && go test -count=1 -timeout 5m -v -run "^TestHostCertStep$" ./internal/agent/apply/'` and record its FAIL lines. Expected there: `--- FAIL: TestHostCertStep`, `TestHostCertStep/hostcert_answer_draining_ca_accepted`, `/hostcert_kept_cert_of_draining_ca_kept_until_due` and `/hostcert_kept_cert_of_drained_ca_replaced` (internal/agent/apply/hostcert_test.go:462, :496, :506), each with the helper `installedByOld`'s message `an answer signed by the draining host CA key before its end: ... want it installed` (:449-454). The e2e kill at step 6a's attempts assertion is the named one; the unit run is the backstop.
- **The PASS loop's negative proof** (a CI guard): the fifth verify command.

No later step edits this file.

**Gates.** Run the test-time delta gate (the fifth verify command) and the full gate (the sixth), each in the background with nothing else running in WSL. If this session or WSL boot is not the one that measured the base, re-measure the base on origin/main in a detached worktree outside the repository first, and replace the line in place. `scripts/capslock-check.sh` must report no new pair (this plan changes no binary's code).

**Push, the PR, the lanes, the tracking update and the review.**
1. Before every commit after P4 (the tracking-and-SUMMARY commit and every fix-round commit), stage by path, the SUMMARY included, and scan the staged added lines with `( D=$(set -o pipefail; git diff -U0 --cached | grep '^+' | grep -v '^+++') && test -n "$D" && test -r "$OWNER_DENY_CS" && test -r "$OWNER_DENY_CI" || exit 1; T='find[i]ng|FIX[-]PLAN|security[-]audit[-]skill|scratch[p]ad|App[D]ata|run[-]1|([0-9]{1,3}\.){3}[0-9]{1,3}'; CSP() { grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#' "$OWNER_DENY_CS" | grep .; }; CIP() { grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#' "$OWNER_DENY_CI" | grep .; }; nomatch() { grep -q "$@"; case $? in 0) return 1;; 1) return 0;; *) echo "a pattern scan could not run" >&2; exit 2;; esac; }; hits() { grep "$@"; test $? -le 1 || { echo "a pattern scan could not run" >&2; return 2; }; }; test -n "$(CSP)" && test -n "$(CIP)" || exit 1; H=$(printf '%s\n' "$D" | sed -E 's/127\.0\.0\.[0-9]{1,3}//g' | hits -iE "$T") || exit 1; test -z "$H" || exit 1; printf '%s\n' "$D" | nomatch -f <(CSP) || exit 1; printf '%s\n' "$D" | nomatch -i -f <(CIP) || exit 1 )`, which must exit 0 before the commit and the push. Every scan of this plan that reads the owner's patterns feeds them through process substitution, with no temp file (03-12f's deviation 5), reads them as basic regular expressions, as this scan always has, and requires grep's exit status to be 0 or 1, so that a pattern grep cannot read fails the scan instead of passing it (`nomatch` and `hits`): an extended-regex reading of a pattern with a literal `(` exits 2, which a bare `!` would turn into a pass. Commit by path only, signed and authored as Daniel. Push with `env -u GITHUB_TOKEN -u GH_TOKEN git push -u origin p03/12f2-ca-rotation-host-ca-e2e`.
2. The PR (`scripts/gh-as-bot.sh pr create --title "test(e2e): prove the host-CA rotation gate against real sshd and real clients"`). The body is neutral, lists the changes and names the limits, each on one line:
   - TestHostCARotation in the existing e2e-rotation lane (OpenSSH 9.5p1 and 10.5p1): one host at a 90 s host cap, a client that refreshed after ceremony 1 and one that did not, a real `keyroster known-hosts sync`, ceremony 1, ceremony 2 with `--ca-switch host`, the switch install with `--previous`, serve's refusal, retire-key, doctor, and the host's own lazy renewal; the stale client is refused with `Host key verification failed` after the renewal and only then;
   - the shared rotation-test helpers `caRotRetireKey` and `caRotDrainRE` take the role, with the user test's behaviour unchanged;
   - the lane's `-run` list, PASS loop and comment, and CONTRIBUTING's two lane rows;
   - ca-rotation.md states the owner's host-CA gate with the tools as they are: ceremony 2 for the host role no earlier than ceremony 1's install plus the user profile's max TTL, which expires every user certificate issued before that install under the policy then in force, so that a user holding only such certificates needs a new one, from a login or from an admin, and which does not show that a login refreshed; which accounts it concerns, and that every one of them, a user who always passes `--no-known-hosts` included, has been refreshed since ceremony 1's install; that an account has refreshed when its managed `keyroster_known_hosts` holds the new host CA's line (`keyroster host CA generation <g>`), which a user gets from a `keyroster login`; that `keyroster known-hosts sync` is an admin's and needs an admin key; that an account with neither (automation) is refreshed by an admin running sync as that account, or it cannot be rotated safely; that `keyroster host status` must read `bundle v<N> on <M>/<M> hosts` for the server's current bundle N, ceremony 1's or a later one that still lists the new host CA; that the gate is the operator's because no tool counts refreshed clients; when hosts move (an agent-renewed host at its own renewal, a host certificate issued by hand only when an admin issues a new one; "Pre-switch check" now says, as the code does, that such a certificate is capped by the host profile and covered by the switch's floor, and that `<e>` lies past a floor only for a certificate issued under an earlier policy with a larger cap) and where the old host CA drains; and names the test. enroll-user.md item 4a, machine-identities.md (machine renew does not refresh host trust; an automation account needs an admin's sync between the two installs) and signer-install.md (the host role's draining key is trusted by the clients and the agents until its end) agree; every new sentence is pinned but two, each pin with its mutant, and ca-rotation.md names no plan number but 03-13 and 03-16;
   - no product code changes, no new required check (the ruleset still lists 30; no job was added or renamed; the rotation job keeps its name, matrix and timeouts);
   - the main e2e lanes do not compile the new test (its build tags are `e2e && e2erotation`);
   - this plan's text, replacing the early text on main;
   - UNVERIFIED, plainly: the host drain's end on a real host and client (unit-tested only), a real fleet, a client on Windows, clients older than OpenSSH 8.5, real clients that never refresh, a sync that an admin runs for another account, a host that has not taken the bundle naming the new host CA when it renews (unit-tested only, and no test shows that it keeps its certificate through that refusal), and a host certificate issued by hand across a host-CA switch; the test does not wait the gate's time;
   - the budget: the rotation lanes' typical, slowest-observed and all-limits figures (about 681/672 s, 860/851 s and 1055/1046 s), with the owner's 900 s rule applied to the measured run.
   Do not enable auto-merge, now or later.
3. **The tracking update, once, now that the PR exists.** Copy ROADMAP.md and STATE.md outside the repository first. Then GSD's tracking update (`state.advance-plan`, `state.update-progress`, `roadmap.update-plan-progress 3`), which resets the counts from the files: ROADMAP's Plans line `39/44 plans executed in 43 waves` becomes `40/44 plans executed in 43 waves`, the progress row `| 39/44 |` becomes `| 40/44 |`, and this plan's row is ticked; STATE's `completed_plans` rises by one from Task 1's reading (74 at c6c632d), `total_plans` stays (79), and `Plan: 39 of 44` becomes `Plan: 40 of 44`. In 03-12f's run the same commands wrote `in 42 waves` and `Plan: 39 of 43` and two scoped edits put `in 43 waves` and `of 44` back (03-12f-SUMMARY.md, "The tracking update"); so after the commands, compare both files with the copies, and wherever the tool wrote a wave count other than `in 43 waves` or a plan total other than `of 44`, put the right text back with one scoped edit each and record it in the SUMMARY. Then one scoped edit (`Edit`, never a whole-file write) replaces the single `stopped_at:` line with `stopped_at: "Completed 03-12f2-PLAN.md (PR #N)"`, N being this plan's PR (`scripts/gh-as-bot.sh pr view p03/12f2-ca-rotation-host-ca-e2e --json number --jq .number`). Read both diffs against the copies in full.
4. Check that GSD ticked only this plan's row (03-12f's deviation 20: a prefix ticks a longer name): of the 44 rows `- [.] 03-` (Task 1's reading), only `03-12f2`'s may differ from origin/main's ROADMAP, which the third verify command proves by comparing the whole file with the intended one. If another row changed, restore it with a scoped edit, record it in the SUMMARY and run the gate again. Then write the SUMMARY as the paragraph below says, with its "Pre-merge review" section marked pending; stage by path, read the staged diffs in full, run step 1's staged-line scan; commit `docs(03-12f2): complete ca-rotation-host-ca-e2e plan` and push.
5. Wait for the required checks and run the first verify command (the required checks, the five named checks and the house e2e gate in `MODE=pr`) and the second (the lane gate); record in the SUMMARY both rotation lanes' and both main lanes' `go test` times beside the base readings ("The budget"), TestHostCARotation's duration in each lane, and the run IDs. **If an e2e-rotation lane's `go test` reads above 900 s, stop and report; no timeout is raised.** A main lane above 900 s is reported as drift with the lane gate's proof that it did not run TestHostCARotation.
6. The orchestrator then runs the pre-merge review on the green PR, before Task 5, and Task 5 does not start until the review is recorded or the owner says to skip it. The review is lean by default, scaled to a test-and-docs change: one lens on the test (does each step's assertion fail on the misbehaviour it names; is every bound taken from the previous issuance; can any login meet another certificate than it claims; can the test pass with the lane filtering it out), one lens on the runbook text (is every sentence true by its anchor, does any text of the sweep still state the gate differently or not at all, is anything claimed that the test does not show), and, at the orchestrator's choice, one Codex lens on `git diff origin/main...HEAD` and the PR's text only, never a ceremony folder, a passphrase file, `~/.ssh` or any planning file outside the repository. Its results, the fix rounds and their checks go in the SUMMARY under "Pre-merge review", written from the review's run record; if the owner merges first, the SUMMARY says that the review ran after the merge or did not run.

**The SUMMARY** contains: the PR number and the IDs of the CI runs that exist when it is written (a run for the SUMMARY's own commit cannot be named in it; the review's docs commit adds the head it reviewed); the plan file's size and sha256 line; BASE, origin/main's SHA at Task 1, and the re-read of the anchors with every difference; G2, R3, W3 and P4 with the statement, per commit, that `git diff --cached` was read in full after the last edit; the wrapper's self-check, both pre-check runs (exit, times, TestHostCARotation's logged serials, gaps, stop windows, the commit lags of bundles 2 and 3 and `Server host certificate` lines), and the same commit lags from both CI lanes; R3's RED and its controls; every mutant (M352, M361, M362 and M368 to M437: the three e2e mutants M371 to M373 and the 70 document mutants) under the mutant rule with the sha256 and `git status` records, and M373's unit run; the sentence that the planning brief's M352 row is the earlier text of the same mutant, not a second definition; the PASS loop's negative proof with its three exit codes; Task 1's base check of the lane gate; the `test-time base:` line and the delta gate's reading; the full gate; the lane gate's output and the five lanes' times against the base; the totals after the tracking update; the publish scan; what stays UNVERIFIED, in the words of this plan; the pre-merge review; `requirements-completed` left empty: KEY-08 completes in 03-13.
  </action>

## Trust Boundaries

| Boundary | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| the runbook → the operator | the host-CA gate is the operator's: no tool counts refreshed clients or holds the host role's switch back |
| the trust bundle → the clients | a client learns a new host CA only through its own refresh; narrowing never adds a line |
| the test → the runner's home | the test writes the real `~/.ssh` managed files of the runner (CI) or of a private mount inside the namespace (local), never the owner's own `~/.ssh` |

## STRIDE Threat Register

Threat IDs are unique within a phase. This plan defines no new threat ID; it carries legs of the rows named, each defined in the plan named, and Task 1 checks that no row from T-03-465 on exists.

| Threat ID | Category | Component | Severity | Disposition | Mitigation Plan |
|-----------|----------|-----------|----------|-------------|-----------------|
| T-03-417 (defined in 03-12d-PLAN.md:816) | Denial of service | a host-CA switch before clients refreshed | medium | mitigate | the end-to-end leg: TestHostCARotation shows the failure the gate prevents (a client that did not refresh is refused at the host's renewal, M371) and the success it allows (a refreshed client across both certificates, M372), and that the hosts move lazily (M373); the gate stated in every runbook text that says when a host-CA switch is safe, pinned (M352, M361, M362, M368 to M370, M374 to M437). Residual, as the runbook says: the gate is the operator's, and a client that never refreshes cannot be rotated safely |
| T-03-SC | Tampering | dependencies | low | mitigate | no module added (go.mod and go.sum unchanged, checked in Task 4), no package-manager install by an executor (the OpenSSH builds are the lane's own, built by scripts/build-openssh.sh from pinned, verified tarballs, already present in WSL), and the namespace wrapper stays outside the repository |

(T-03-430, a drained host CA staying in a client's known_hosts, defined in 03-12d-PLAN.md:818, is untouched: the test ends before the drain, and narrowing never removes the generation-1 line during it.)
</threat_model>

## Edge Coverage (spec-less fallback, this plan)

**Flagged assumptions.**
- **Loopback stands in for a fleet**: one host, two clients, one OpenSSH build per lane; real fleets and clients stay UNVERIFIED.
- **The `Server host certificate` line names the signing CA's fingerprint on both builds**: read first in the pre-check, with a stop if it does not.
- **A host-only bundle commits within its bound**: 45 s after the install for bundle 2, and by r1's issuance plus 44 s for bundle 3, 21 s after the latest serve for a path of about 20 s counted from the code ("The test's timing", Back-off); it renders the same files (castate.go:102), so no widening wait applies; INFERRED from the code and bounded, the user switch's commit in about 11 s being an upper reading that includes a KRL change.

**Edges:** an anchor certificate observed late (one re-anchor per test, then fail); an early request after the switch (step 6a's attempts assertion, whatever the signer answers; then inside the signer's recent window r1 re-delivered and refused, the log assertion, or after it a new certificate, the drop-in check, and step 7's gap last; step 6 ends inside the window and cannot see it); a late `hcrNext` call that meets a later renewal (its own message, naming the late call); a user whose login did not refresh host trust; an automation account that nobody refreshes; a host that did not take ceremony 1's bundle (unit-tested); a renewal that could meet a stopped signer (both deadlines of each stop); the drain binding with a renewal between issued_at and the install (allowance 60 s, stop by issued_at plus 50 s); an eager host move (M373); B refreshed (M371); A without the new line (M372); a skipped or filtered test (the PASS loop, the lane gate); the main lanes compiling the test (the tag line, `go list`, the lane gate's main-lane leg); a run over 900 s (stop and report); narrowing during the test (it never adds and the generation-1 line stays trusted until bundle 3's issued_at plus 450 s); the runner's real `~/.ssh` (requireRealHome refuses existing files and removes its own; locally a private mount).

## Lessons from 03-12e2, 03-12f and PR #113, and where this plan applies them

- **A gate that reads a fetched log drops the echoed scripts first, and is proven on the base log with controls** (03-12f's deviation 21): "The lane gate and its proof"; Task 1 runs it on the base push run.
- **Run every gate against the base tree** (03-12e2's deviation 10): "Gate base values at c6c632d".
- **A test that only a lane runs is still proven before the lane**: the local pre-check through a namespace wrapper on both OpenSSH versions (03-12e2's local run), and its setup and product mutants (carry-in item 8).
- **A local e2e run must not touch the owner's home**: the wrapper mounts a private directory over `~/.ssh`, because the test writes the real home by design.
- **A tool's early exit is not a clean run** (03-12f's review, X2): doctor must exit 0 and print the line only its last block prints.
- **An operator rule is stated in every text, with a pin** (03-12f's review, custody.md): "The sweep".
- **A sentence about the code is checked against the code before it is written** (03-12f's deviation 22): the anchor table of "The sweep" and Task 3's stop rule.
- **A prefix is not a number**: `03-12f` and `03-12f2`; the exact-file ROADMAP gate.
- **A read-only checkout stays read-only**: `go list` was run in the read-only checkout through WSL and `git status` stayed clean after it.
